The IME's output is one `IMEEvent` per atomic update (text-input-v3 `done`, one `WM_IME_COMPOSITION`, one `NSTextInputClient` call), applied as delete surrounding bytes → commit → new preedit with its cursor (both -1: hidden), delivered only when it changes something; an empty preedit without a commit cancels a composition. `IMESessionEvent{Active}` reports the session (focus, a state, the OS attaching the IME); after it ends a composition gets nothing more. All offsets are UTF-8 bytes; Win32 and macOS convert from UTF-16 (`internal/platform/internal/utf16x`). Keys the IME consumes never arrive; `KeyProcess` keys and `KeyEvent.Text` during a composition are not inserted. Wayland: `zwp_text_input_v3` version 1, one per seat; content-type changes restart the session (disable, enable); change cause "other" on enable and reset (GTK); stale `done` serials are applied as the protocol says and the state is never held back waiting for a matching serial; surrounding text over 4000 bytes is cut to a window around the selection. events `OnBeforeInput(func(tela.InputEvent))` (`InputType` with W3C names, `Data`, `IsComposing`; `PreventDefault` cancels, except during a composition), `OnChange(func(tela.ChangeEvent))` (`Value`, `InputType`, `Data`, `IsComposing`), `OnCompositionStart/Update/End(func(tela.CompositionEvent))` (`Data`), `OnSelect(func(tela.SelectEvent))` (`Start`, `End`: UTF-8 bytes; fired when the user makes or changes a non-empty selection and when code changes it), `OnSubmit(func(tela.SubmitEvent))`, all bubbling; `Type` (Input: text, password, email, search, url, tel, number; Button: submit, button, reset; unknown values panic in dev mode with a suggestion), `Rows`, `Placeholder`, `ReadOnly`, `MaxLength` (UTF-16 code units, user edits only), `AutoComplete` and `SpellCheck` (accepted, ignored), `Name`, `Id`, `For`; An Input centers its line in its content box (its baseline), clips it to the content box and scrolls it horizontally itself to keep the caret visible; a Textarea is a scroll container whose extents come from its text, so a keystroke shapes the field's paragraph and records its layer (a `layerField`) without laying the tree out. Text fields match `focus-visible:` on mouse focus; Tab selects the whole value, a programmatic or label focus keeps the selection (initially the start). | 070 | 2026-10-13 | Phase-4 API gaps (phase 5, T9; the API is the owner's decision). `ElementRef.Bounds() tela.Rect` is the element's border box in window coordinates as painted: moved by the scroll offsets of its containers and by its own and its ancestors' transforms, as the axis-aligned box of the transformed box (`getBoundingClientRect`; an inline element has the union of its fragments); zero before mount, when `display: none` or before layout. `tela.Rect` is new, `{X, Y, Width, Height float32}` with `Right`, `Bottom`, `Contains` (an alias of `core.Rect`; `gfx.Rect` keeps its corners). `tela.UseBounds(ref) *Value[Rect]` is updated by the Tree at the end of every frame that ran the pipeline (outside `Frame`, like `UseScroll`'s values) when the bounds changed, so its readers (a build, a `WidthFn`) run in the next frame, one frame after the change, and a frame that moves nothing requests none; because one frame is not enough for a one-shot render, `core.Render` and the headless entry points of `telatest` (so `tela snapshot` and `inspect`) run frames until the tree has no work, at most 8 (state set by layout effects and by passive effects of the first frame is now rendered too). `h.If[C tela.Child](cond, c C) C` returns `c` or the zero value, so it takes nodes and props (the zero `Prop` is a no-op, also in `Fragment` and `Provider`, which used to panic on it); `h.IfElse`; `h.MapIndex(items, func(i, item) Node)`. `Value.Latest()` is the applied value with every pending `Set` and `Update` applied in queue order: each Value keeps its pending operations, appended under a lock together with the update so both orders agree; `Update` functions run again on the caller's goroutine (they must be pure and must not call `Latest` of their own Value); it never subscribes and is safe from any goroutine; `Get` is unchanged. `h.TextFn(fn)` is a text node whose function is evaluated when the box phase assembles its paragraph, with its reads tracked (`obsText`): a change reassembles that paragraph, which is shaped and laid out only if the text changed; no build, no style. `tela inspect --layout` and `telatest.LayoutJSON` keep `rect` (layout, no scroll, no transform) and add `bounds` (same shape; scroll and transforms applied) only when it differs from `rect` by more than 1/1000 px. CSS border triangles already drew like Chromium's (adjacent sides are cut along the diagonal from the outer to the inner corner, ADR-045); the bug was in unequal widths: the wedge of a side extended past the inner edge and painted over the opposite side, so a side's region is now bounded by its inner edge, moved inwards by the inner corner radius (Blink's quad of a side). `space-x-*` and `space-y-*` are no longer gaps (supersedes the "gap in flex containers" part of ADR-044): `ComputedStyle.SpaceX/SpaceY` are lengths, negative values allowed (`-space-x-2`), that become the inline-end / block-end margin of every child element but the last (`:where(& > :not(:last-child))`, DOM sense: a trailing `display: none` or absolute child still counts) in block and flex containers alike, and independent of `gap-*`; a margin of the child on that side wins (the rule has no specificity; an explicit `mr-0` cannot be told from none); a positive `space-x-*` between the inline elements of a paragraph stays an empty inline box and a negative one is ignored there | Checked against Chromium 152 with Tailwind 4.3.3 pages (`testdata/chromium/borderjoin`, `space`: 7 rows of triangles, pinwheels, rounded and translucent joins, and 9 rows of avatars, block, flex, wrap, justify, own margins, hidden last child, at scales 1 and 2): geometry within 0.01 px, pixels within 63 levels at join seams (mean ≤ 0.23); before the fix a side of a box with widths 6/12/18/3 painted 208 levels off. `UseBounds` costs one comparison per follower per frame; a subscription would need layout-time hooks in `layout`. Margins instead of gaps because only margins reproduce Tailwind v4 in wrapped, `justify-between` and column flex containers |
| 071 | 2026-10-13 | Win32 IME through IMM32 (phase 5; implements ADR-068 on Windows). A new window has no input context; a non-nil, non-password `TextInputState` associates the thread's default one (`ImmAssociateContextEx` `IACE_DEFAULT`), nil and the password purpose remove it, so the session (`IMESessionEvent`) is focus and an associated context, and is never active in a password field; other purposes and `Multiline` leave the IME alone (no forced open status or conversion mode). `SetTextInput`/`ResetTextInput` are recorded and applied once at the end of the delivery pass. Ending a session during a composition marks it over first and then completes it in the IME (`ImmNotifyIME` `CPS_COMPLETE`, Chromium on blur), so the result is not delivered and the shared default context keeps no composition; `ResetTextInput` forgets the composition and then cancels it (`CPS_CANCEL`). `WM_IME_SETCONTEXT` clears `ISC_SHOWUICOMPOSITIONWINDOW`; `WM_IME_STARTCOMPOSITION` and `WM_IME_COMPOSITION` never reach `DefWindowProc` and `WM_IME_CHAR` is dropped, so results are never typed again as `WM_CHAR`. One `WM_IME_COMPOSITION` is one `IMEEvent` (result → `Commit`, composition string → `Preedit`); the preedit cursor is the target clause of `GCS_COMPATTR` as a highlighted range (winit, Chromium), else `GCS_CURSORPOS`, else hidden; `WM_IME_ENDCOMPOSITION` with a preedit shown cancels it (a Korean result after it is a plain `Commit`). `VK_PROCESSKEY` presses never carry `Text`. The cursor area becomes, in physical client pixels, the composition window (`CFS_POINT`), the candidate window (`CFS_CANDIDATEPOS` plus `CFS_EXCLUDE`, none for Chinese IMEs, Korean one pixel lower) and a hidden system caret for Chinese and Japanese IMEs, re-sent on area, scale and input-language changes and at each composition start (Chromium's `MoveImeWindow`); `IMR_QUERYCHARPOSITION` answers the cursor area, `IMR_DOCUMENTFEED` 256 characters each side with the preedit, `IMR_RECONVERTSTRING` the selection when there is one and no composition (Chromium) | Modern IMEs are TSF services that Windows bridges to IMM32 (CUAS), so IMM32 reaches all of them with a fraction of TSF's COM surface; completing a composition the app already kept is the only order that neither types it twice nor leaves it in the shared context; the GCS → event logic is pure (`ime.go`) and tested on every OS, the OS half is checked live on Windows 11 with Microsoft IME (Japanese) |
| 072 | 2026-10-13 | Phase-5 review fixes (amends ADR-050, ADR-067, ADR-069). **Alt chords type nothing** on Windows and Linux: a text field inserts a key's `Text` only without Ctrl, Alt and Meta (Option still types on macOS; AltGr is never Alt). **Alt codes on Windows:** a press with Alt and no `WM_SYSCHAR` (the numpad digits of an Alt code) has no `Text`; the `WM_CHAR` that `TranslateMessage` posts with the release of Alt after a numpad key pressed with Alt is delivered after that release as a press and release of their own with an unidentified Code (0x10000 plus the first code unit, as `VK_PACKET` text) and the character as `Key` and `Text`, without Alt in `Mods`. **IME deletions:** `beforeinput` for an IME's delete-surrounding fires before the deletion (deleteContentBackward, deleteContentForward or deleteContent; `IsComposing` during a composition) and `PreventDefault` does not cancel it. **IDs:** a duplicate `Id` names the first mounted element in tree order (HTML's `getElementById`; it was the last one mounted), and the others take over when it unmounts; mounted elements are indexed by `Id` and Labels by `For`. **Editor:** switching to single-line always resets the undo history | Chromium inserts nothing for Alt chords (Windows sends them as `WM_SYSCHAR`, GTK treats them as accelerators) and types the Alt-code `WM_CHAR` as a char event; the KeyEvent contract keeps `Text` on presses, so the character gets its own press rather than riding on the Alt release. | 073 | 2026-10-18 | CSS grid (phase 6, T1). `internal/layout` ports Taffy v0.14.0's grid (`src/compute/grid/`) into `grid*.go`: explicit grid with line names and `repeat()` (`auto-fill`/`auto-fit` counted from the size, max size or min size), implicit tracks (`grid-auto-rows/columns`, cycled), placement (sparse and dense, both flows, spans, negative and named lines, 10,000-track limit), the track sizing algorithm, content alignment and self alignment (baseline included in rows). `Style` gains `DisplayGrid`, a `Grid *GridTemplate` (track lists, auto tracks, named areas; compared by pointer, so core keeps the pointer while `GridTemplate.Equal` holds), `GridAutoFlow`, `JustifyItems`, `GridRow`/`GridColumn` placements and `JustifySelf`; Dim gains `UnitFr`. Conformance: Taffy's `grid`, `blockgrid` and `gridflex` fixtures measured by Chromium 152 like the others (all 553 included ones match within 0.5 px; the excluded ones are vertical writing modes and margin collapsing), plus Tela cases for named lines and areas, auto-repeat, dense placement, static positions, baselines and content-sized grids. Where Chromium and Taffy disagree, layout follows Chromium: an auto-width grid under definite available space is fit-content (sized under max-content and min-content constraints) and an auto height is a max-content constraint (CSS Grid 1 11.1); size queries include the second column/row passes; a track holding a span-1 item gets a finite growth limit even for zero contributions; beyond-limit distribution caps fit-content() only for max-content contributions and counts auto as max-content; the automatic minimum looks at the spanned tracks and the other axis' min size does not transfer through the aspect ratio; items with an aspect ratio and an auto width are measured with a row estimate sized first; named lines follow CSS Grid 1 8.3; the container's first baseline is its first item's. Absolutely positioned children follow ADR-043's containing blocks: a positioned grid lays them out in their grid area (auto lines at the padding edges) with CSS 2's rules, a static grid gives them a static position in its content box aligned by `justify-self`/`align-self`. `core` maps grid and inline-grid onto it; flex and grid containers share the item structure (element children are items, text runs anonymous items). Classes reach line names in arbitrary track lists and named placements (`col-[main]`, `col-start-[main]`, `col-span-[name]`) plus Tailwind v4's `col-N`/`-col-start-N`; `grid-template-areas` and `grid-area` have no Tailwind utility, so area templates are not reachable from classes | Grid is in §9's scope; Taffy is the reference whose fixtures Chromium agrees with on all but script-driven ones, and the deviations come from probing Chromium where the fixtures (or Tela's own context, which sizes grids inside real containers instead of at the root) disagree with Taffy. Keeping templates behind one pointer keeps `layout.Style` comparable, which core's restyle relies on |
| 074 | 2026-10-18 | Blend modes, mask layers and focal gradients (phase 6; implements the `blend` of §13's `PushLayer` and amends ADR-032). **Blend modes:** every `gfx.BlendMode` composites a layer onto its backdrop per W3C Compositing and Blending Level 1 (plus-lighter: min(1, s + d) on every channel, Level 2) on sRGB-encoded premultiplied 8-bit values (ADR-007): the group's pixels are scaled by its opacity and mask, blended, and the coverage of the clip active at `PushLayer` interpolates between the backdrop and the result (CSS's order filter → mask → opacity → blend; Skia's coverage rule for modes that cannot fold it into the source). Multiply, screen, overlay, darken, lighten, hard-light, difference and exclusion are exact integer expressions rounded once; color-dodge, color-burn, soft-light and the non-separable modes (Lum 0.3/0.59/0.11) are fusion-safe float64. The layer's bounds are cut by the mask's; a nil or empty mask drops the block; `gfx.Equal` compares masks command by command, and `core`'s scroll shifts treat masked layers as not shift-exact. The mask belongs to a layer instead of being a clip so that overlapping content is masked as one group: CSS `mask-image`, `background-clip: text` (the backgrounds in a layer masked by the element's glyph runs) and COLR SrcIn/DestIn all need that. **Focal gradients:** `gfx.FocalGradient{Focal, FocalRadius, Center, Radius, Stops, Interp}` is the two-point conical gradient of Canvas, SVG (fx, fy, fr) and COLRv1: a point takes the largest t whose circle passes through it with r(t) ≥ 0, offsets beyond [0, 1] pad, points outside the cone are transparent; t is a quadratic's root computed without cancellation. **COLR:** `internal/text` drops `exactBlend`, the source-over recoloring of blend modes, the outline-union clip of non-solid SrcIn masks and the banded-circle emulation: blend composites are blend layers, SrcIn/DestIn with a non-solid mask are mask layers, and two-point gradients are focal gradients between circles of their family at the ends of the color line (moved to the circle of radius 0 where radii turn negative). SrcOut, DestOut, Xor and the atop modes stay approximate (Noto Color Emoji does not use them) | Measured against Chromium 152 (`internal/gfx/raster` `TestChromiumScenes4`, scales 1 and 2): 18 `mix-blend-mode` cells over opaque, translucent and out-of-gamut gradient backdrops (isolated groups, and one under a rounded clip) within 0.57 mean levels per channel; 6 `mask-image` cells (alpha, luminance, radial, nested, under a rounded clip and a rotation, with opacity and multiply) within 0.49; 8 canvas two-point gradients (focal, focal radius, on the edge, cone, strip, shrinking, ellipse) within 0.15; maxima 3–5 levels inside cells (16 for color-dodge, whose result changes fastest near Cs = 1) and up to 44 on anti-aliased rounded or rotated edges. `background-clip: text` built as a gradient in a layer masked by Inter and emoji glyph runs (`internal/text` `TestChromiumClipText`) differs only on glyph edges (means 0.47–3.48 per line, anti-aliasing and ADR-048's baselines at scale 2). | 076 | 2026-10-18 | Animation layer and implicit transitions (phase 6, T4; implements §12 "Clock", "Implicit transitions" and the predefined keyframe classes of §10; refines ADR-061 for animated transform and opacity). **`internal/anim`** (below `core`, no tree knowledge): `Ticker` and `Animator` (frame clock only, never `time.Now`; `Running` and `Endless` states so that owners ask for frames and test runners know what to wait for), `Curve` (`CubicBezier` with the CSS and Tailwind v4 curves, `Steps`, `Linear`, `ParseEasing`), `Spring` (Motion's model in closed form, `SpringForDuration` = Motion's `findSpring`, rest detection; ported, MIT), typed interpolation (`LerpColor`, `LerpShadows`, `LerpTransform`, `LerpFilters`, lengths) with `Prop`, `Equal`, `CanInterpolate`, `Blend` over `ComputedStyle` groups (the bits of `style.TransitionProps`), `Transition` with CSS's reversing rule, `Spec` (the `animation` shorthand and the Web Animations timing model), `Keyframes` and the built-in spin, ping, pulse and bounce (Tailwind v4's keyframes). Everything is fusion-safe (ADR-041, `fmath`). **Style model:** `Compute`'s result is the immutable target; while a box animates `Box.cs` is a copy of it with the animated groups replaced by their value at the frame time (`Box.anim`: running transitions, keyframe run), so layout, paint and hit testing need no changes; `Tree.Animate(now)` (phase 2, the Host passes the FrameEvent time) advances the ticker and each frame installs a new copy. **Transitions** follow CSS Transitions Level 1 (start from the current value; a running one whose target is unchanged continues; a new target replaces it, or reverses it with the shortening factor when it is the start value; no matching `transition-property` or no combined duration cancels; no transition at mount). A tree that is not driven by `Animate` (`Render`, direct `Frame`) has no transitions and shows keyframes at 0. Reduced motion removes transitions (running ones end); `motion-reduce:animate-none` is the way out of keyframes, as Tailwind does. Inherited groups (color, font size) restyle children, which follow the parent's current value and start no transition for them (nor for border, outline and decoration colors that follow the text color). **Invalidation by property class:** opacity and transform (layer) only `markPaint` the box, which is a deferred stacking context for as long as it animates (a no-op paint-phase deferred prop plus `stacking`), so a frame costs no build, restyle, layout or placement and ADR-061's damage rasterizes the old and new areas; shadow lists paired by Tailwind's slots (inset shadow, inset ring, ring, shadow) before padding with transparent zero shadows, so a ring grows instead of morphing into the shadow; transforms per component (translate, rotate, scale, skew), keyframe transforms composed on the base by adding translations and angles and multiplying scales (CSS's order for translate, rotate and scale, exact unless a rotated or scaled base meets an animated translation). **Frames and idle:** after every frame `afterFrame` asks for another while the ticker is active, so an app is idle when the last transition ends; infinite animations ask while mounted; one inside `display: none` is dormant and restarts when shown (CSS); boxes that are visible but off screen keep animating (frames paint and rasterize nothing); `Tree.OnlyEndlessAnimations` lets `telatest` skip the wait for infinite animations (it waits for transitions and finite animations, advancing the clock). **Not here:** `steps()` and `linear()` in classes, custom `@keyframes` (Go API, T9), animating `min/max-*`, gradients, `width: auto`. | A browser's model, since users and LLMs write `transition` and `animate-spin` expecting it, and the oracle is Chromium: 11 cases at four times (oklch and hex colors, white to black, opacity, translate, scale and rotate, ring and shadow, width, blur, spin, ping, pulse, bounce) differ from its screenshots by a mean of 0.007 to 0.44 levels per cell and by at most 3 (blur) to 71 (spin) levels, the largest on anti-aliased edges (flat interiors within a few levels). Spin and hover frames of 100 buttons: 0.15-0.24 ms (x1) and 0.26-0.41 ms (x2) for a frame with one animate-spin element, 0.9-1.3 ms and 1.1-1.8 ms per frame while buttons with the bare `transition` are hovered in sequence (about 60 % of it raster of the shadows); incremental frames equal full ones in a property test of spinners, hover and size transitions |
| 077 | 2026-10-18 | Fixed and sticky positioning, portals and the color scheme (phase 6, T8; implements §9's `fixed`/`sticky` and §13's portal layer, whose `tela.Portal` becomes `h.Portal`; amends ADR-043's containing blocks and ADR-061's chunks; the public API is the owner's decision). **Containing blocks** (Chromium): a box with a transform, a filter or a backdrop filter contains its absolute and fixed descendants (`layout.Style.ContainingBlock`, `containsPositioned` in paint.go). What counts is stable between restyles, so that layout and paint agree: the target style of the classes and props (ADR-076; a transition counts by its end value, so one back to no transform does not count while it runs, where Chromium would), plus an attached keyframe animation of the transform (`animate-spin`, like Chromium's running transform animations); a paint-phase `TranslateFn` alone does not count, nor does an identity transform (`translate-x-0`, which Chromium's `translate: 0 0` would). **Fixed**: `layout.Style.Fixed` on an absolute node; the container algorithms record its static position and skip it; after every full layout of a node, `fixedPass` looks at its children for fixed nodes and for subtrees that report escaping ones (`output.escapesFixed`), and the root or a ContainingBlock node lays them out by walking down to them, so the container algorithms only record the static position of a fixed child and skip it (block, flex and grid; ADR-073's grid also uses `absContainer`/`passesAbs`, so a transformed grid contains its absolute children); a fixed node laid out by the root adds nothing to its scrollable overflow. A sticky chunk always carries its translation, and the damage never shifts a sticky space as a scrolled unit (its offset is not how its pixels move): its commands are matched. | 078 | 2026-10-18 | Fast large blurs; parallel, damage-aware backdrop filters (phase 6, T5; amends ADR-028's blur passes, ADR-045's "a list that uses it rasterizes on one goroutine" and ADR-061's backdrop closure). **Downsampled blur:** a device standard deviation s gets a factor f, the largest power of two (at most 64) whose full-resolution support 3f(m+1) + 3f/2 - 1 plus the rounded offset fits the reach 3s + \|offset\| (from about s = 21; f is about s/10). The surface is averaged over f x f blocks aligned to device coordinates (8.8 fixed point), the blocks are blurred with three passes of a fractional box (2m+1 samples plus a weight alpha at each end, 24-bit integer weights) whose variance is that of the full-resolution three-box kernel divided by f², minus the 1/4 that averaging and linear interpolation add, and the result is interpolated linearly back to the pixel centers with exact integer weights, rounded once. The last band to arrive computes the filter's output once over the region with a work pool (the blur's rows, column strips and block rows split among goroutines) while the others wait, then every band draws its rows. Other such lists render on one goroutine, with the pool. Truncated Gaussian taps at low resolution were as accurate but cost O(s/f) per value; fractional boxes cost O(1) but need s/f of about 10 to fit the reach. `ClickAt(x, y, ...ClickOption)` and `DragFromTo(from, to tela.Point, ...DragOption)` act at window coordinates in logical pixels with the same real pointer events as `Click` and `Drag` and fail when a point is outside the window; `PixelAt(x, y) color.RGBA` reads the device pixel that contains the logical point, `(floor(x*scale), floor(y*scale))`, of the settled frame (alpha-premultiplied as stored); `Measure(f) Stats` settles, runs `f`, settles like an action (`NoSettle` respected) and returns the field-wise difference of `Stats`; `Expect(loc, states...)` with the `State` values `Checked`, `Pressed`, `Selected`, `Expanded` (a `bool` each; an element without the toggle, or `mixed`, never satisfies it, and the message names the `h.Aria…` prop that sets it), `Disabled`, `Focused`, `HasName` and `HasValue`: it auto-waits like the other expectations, requires one match, and its failure shows the unmet states, the element's actual states and its line of the aria snapshot. `ExpectValue` and `ExpectFocused` stay (`HasValue` and `Focused(true)` overlap them) | Tests stop matching strings of aria snapshots for state, and the common one-component test, a golden per scale and an assertion on how narrow an update was are one call each. A `Rect` instead of four results because it composes (`r.Right()`, `r.Contains`) and is what `ElementRef.Bounds` and `UseBounds` already return. The test id is on the element rather than in the semantics node so that nothing of it can leak to consumers of the tree. `GoldenScales` re-renders the live app instead of mounting a new one so the state a test built up is kept; the pictures equal fresh mounts (checked in a test) |
| 087 | 2026-10-20 | Internationalization (phase 7, T7a; the owner chose the data source, catalog format and API shape; amends ADR-011 and §5). **Package** `i18n` is public, above `tela`/`h` and below `aria`/`kit` (kit components will format dates and numbers); it imports `tela` and `internal/core` (the `EnvDev` constant) only, so the order line of §5 gains `i18n` after `tela/h`. **Dependency:** `golang.org/x/text` (BSD-3, pure Go; v0.43.0, already an indirect dependency of go-text at v0.21.0): `language` for tags, CLDR parents and matching, `feature/plural` for cardinal and ordinal rules, `number` (through `message.Printer`) for decimal and percent formatting; CLDR 32 data, so languages that gained the `many` category since (es, fr, it, pt for 1,000,000) do not have it. **Catalogs** are JSON, one file per locale named after it (`es.json`, `en-US.json`; underscores accepted), with ICU MessageFormat values, nested objects giving dotted keys; `Load(fsys, pattern)` parses every message (own parser: arguments, `number`/`date`/`time` with the short styles, `plural` with `=N`, keywords and `offset`, `selectordinal`, `select`, nesting, `#`, ICU's double-optional apostrophe mode) and fails as a whole with file, key, line and column; skeletons, patterns, currencies, units, spellout and `choice` are rejected at load time. **State** is a process-wide immutable snapshot swapped atomically (like `tela.Assets` it is a global registry: catalogs, fallback, explicit locale), so `T`, `Locale` and the hooks never lock and `Load`, `SetLocale` and `SetFallback` are safe from any goroutine. `Locale()` and `UseLocale()` return a BCP 47 string, not an x/text type, so apps do not depend on that module; the current locale is the explicit one, else the system's (Windows `GetUserDefaultLocaleName`, else `LC_ALL`/`LC_MESSAGES`/`LANG`, first one set decides, `C`/`POSIX` is none) when a catalog covers its language, else the fallback (`en` when loaded, else the first catalog loaded, or `SetFallback`), so the locale of a catalog-less `SetLocale("fr")` is kept for number, date and list formatting. A message is looked up in the locale's catalog, its CLDR parents' (es-MX, es) and the fallback's (matching: exact tag, `Tag.Parent()` chain, then x/text's matcher when the language is the same or the confidence is high); a message from another language than the locale's (the fallback's) is evaluated with that message's locale, so its plural rules and digits fit its text. A missing key returns the key and, with `TELA_DEV=1`, is reported once per locale and key on stderr (also: odd argument lists, a missing or mistyped argument, which keep their `{name}`). **Arguments** are name/value pairs (`t("files", "count", n)`, the `log/slog` convention; one form, no map). **Components:** `UseLocale`, `UseT` and `UseFormatter` are ordinary hooks built from `UseContext`, `UseState` and `UseEffect`, with no change to `core`: the effect subscribes a callback to the package's subscriber list (cleanup unsubscribes) and the callback calls `Setter.Update(func(string) string { return Locale() })`, which applies on the UI thread reading the locale at that moment, so racing `SetLocale` calls end on the last and an equal locale rebuilds nothing; the effect also catches up once after subscribing (a change between build and effect). Only components that call a hook rebuild; `T` and the `Format…` functions are not hooks and never subscribe (documented). **`i18n.Provider(tag, children...)`** (a context) fixes the locale of a subtree and the hooks below it ignore the global locale: the isolation for tests (telatest mounts under a Provider, no new telatest option) and for previews. **`Formatter`** (`For(tag)`, `UseFormatter()`, zero value = current locale) carries `T`, `Number`, `Date`, `Time`, `DateTime`, `List`, `Locale`, `Dir`; the package-level `T`, `FormatNumber`, `FormatDate`, `FormatTime`, `FormatDateTime`, `FormatList` are the zero Formatter's methods. **Dates and lists:** x/text keeps its date tables private and has no list patterns, so `i18n/internal/gen` (`go generate`) reads CLDR 48 JSON vendored per locale in `i18n/internal/gen/cldr` (only the used subtree of `ca-gregorian.json` and `listPatterns.json`, original paths; Unicode License v3, listed in `THIRD_PARTY.md`; `-fetch` refreshes it) and emits `dates_gen.go`: a string pool plus one fixed record per locale (month and weekday names, AM/PM, eras, the four date and time patterns, date-time glue with CLDR's `atTime` form for full and long, the standard list patterns) for 113 locales: 88 languages and 25 regional variants whose patterns differ; other locales resolve by the same parent/matcher rule (en-SG to en-001, es-PE to es-419, zh-TW to zh-Hant), unknown ones use English. The formatter interprets LDML patterns (`G y M L d E c e a b B h H K k m s z v`; the generator fails on any other letter in a standard pattern), Gregorian calendar only (Thai prints CE years with the era), in the `time.Time`'s own zone (zone names are abbreviations or `GMT+1`, not localized), digits in the locale's own numbering system (found by formatting 0 to 9 with x/text, so dates and numbers agree). `DateStyle` is `Short < Medium < Long < Full`. **Direction:** `Dir(tag)` is `RTL` when the tag's script (own or the language's default, `Tag.Script`) is Arabic, Hebrew, Thaana, Syriac, N'Ko, Adlam and a few others; layout mirroring and `rtl:` are phase 7 T7b. **Cost:** importing `i18n` adds about 0.7 MB to an unstripped hello-world Tela binary (23.9 to 24.6 MB; 0.5 MB stripped), most of it x/text's plural and number tables and about 0.1 MB the generated dates | Owner's decisions (x/text, JSON with ICU, the API names); the rest follows the precedents: a global registry like `tela.Assets`, environment hooks like `UseColorScheme` (here from public hooks only, so two other agents could change `core` and `telatest` meanwhile). Subscribing from an effect and reading the global in `Update` costs one list entry per subscribed component and avoids a `core` API; the catch-up and the read-at-apply rule make a missed or reordered notification impossible. A Provider is smaller than a telatest option (it also works in apps) and cannot leak between parallel tests, which a global setter cannot guarantee. Strings for locales keep x/text out of the public API. Generating dates from CLDR (rather than hand-writing patterns) keeps 113 locales exact and updatable; vendoring a trimmed subtree keeps the repository at 0.5 MB instead of about 2 MB. Verified: plural categories for en, es, fr, ru, ar, ja, pl and ordinals; numbers (`1.234,5`, `1 234,5`, `12,34,567`, Arabic-Indic digits); short/medium/long/full dates in en, es, ja, ar and seven more locales; telatest checks that `SetLocale` builds only the components that call `UseT` or `UseLocale` (one build each), from handlers and goroutines, and that Providers isolate subtrees |

Playwright's snapshot is the contract of ADR-058, so the new states are readable in the semantics tree and in `telatest`'s messages but not in the YAML. Pointer events plus a bounds check give React Aria's exit and re-entry rules for mouse, touch and pen with one code path, since the engine's capture makes the element the target of the whole press; the click handler is what lets a Label, an agent or a screen reader press without pointer events. Preventing the click's default and stopping its propagation is React Aria's rule and keeps a pressable card from also clicking its row. Controlled-by-`OnChange` is the rule closest to the owner's example (`Selected: on, OnChange: setOn.Set` versus `DefaultSelected`) and the one a plain bool allows. Sharing one ref is the price of one `desc.ref` per element: a multi-ref engine would touch the focus, scroll and bounds bookkeeping. The pan cancel is the DOM's own rule and the smallest way to give a press the scroll signal the arena already has |

The same rule, `Open *bool`, `DefaultOpen`, `OnOpenChange`, holds for every overlay hook (`boolState` in `aria/state.go`, whose `request` also updates an uncontrolled value at once, so a second request in the same event sees it). No example or test needed a change. (3) `ElementRef.Click()` (public through the `tela.ElementRef` alias) is `element.click()`: a click with the primary button and a click count of 1, at the center of the bounds, made by no pointer (a virtual press for `UsePress`), then the activation behavior of ADR-069 (a Button in a Form submits or resets by its type, a Label focuses its control and clicks it); a disabled or unmounted element ignores it. Called from a handler, an effect or a posted function it must not rebuild under the code that called it, so `Tree.handlerDepth` (every event dispatch and `ApplyUpdates`) and `Tree.inFrame` make `flushNested` do nothing there: the event or frame that runs applies and builds the updates. (4) `UseButton` gains `Type` (`"button"` by default, `"submit"`, `"reset"`; a typo panics in dev mode with the closest value): when a press completes, after `OnPress`, `core.ActivateButton(ref, type)` submits or resets the Form that contains the element, whatever the element is (a Div too; the press still prevents the click's own activation, so a Button is not activated twice); it is the activation of ADR-069, so `ref.Click()` on a hook-made submit button submits through the virtual press. (5) `ToggleOptions` gains `OnPress`, `OnPressStart`, `OnPressEnd`, `OnPressChange`, `OnPressUp`. **Overlay hooks.** Options struct in, result with `Props` out; the overlay is rendered by the caller (in a `Portal`, in a `Presence` to fade out) and the hooks that put props on its element share its ref (`OverlayTriggerResult.OverlayRef` is passed as their `Ref` option; the two-refs panic says so). `UseOverlay(OverlayOptions{Open, OnClose, Dismissable, KeyboardDismissDisabled, ShouldCloseOnBlur, ShouldCloseOnInteractOutside, Modal})` puts `core.Overlay` on the element only while `Open`: Escape calls `OnClose` (not with `KeyboardDismissDisabled`, which still swallows the key); with `Dismissable` a press outside asks `ShouldCloseOnInteractOutside(tela.PointerEvent)` (nil: yes) and, when it will close, is stopped and prevented at the pointer down, and at the release is stopped, prevented (its click is cancelled) and calls `OnClose`, as React Aria's `useOverlay`; `ShouldCloseOnBlur` uses a new engine callback (below); `Modal` is `core.Overlay{Modal}`. `useModalOverlay` is folded into it (`Modal`), and there are no `underlayProps`: a press outside a modal overlay hits no element below (ADR-096), and a scrim is an ordinary element. `UseDialog(DialogOptions{Role, Modal, AriaLabel, Ref})` returns `{Props, TitleProps, Manager, Ref}`: `Props` are a focus scope (`Contain` when modal, `Restore`, `AutoFocus`), the role (`dialog` or `alertdialog`, else a dev panic), `TabIndex(-1)` (so the dialog takes the focus when nothing inside is tabbable), `aria-modal` and the name (`AriaLabel`, else `AriaLabelledBy` the element with `TitleProps`); an alert dialog is an overlay that is not `Dismissable`. `UseOverlayTrigger(OverlayTriggerOptions{Type, Open, DefaultOpen, OnOpenChange, Disabled, Ref, OverlayRef})` returns `{TriggerProps, OverlayProps, Open, SetOpen, Toggle, Close, Pressed, Ref, OverlayRef}`: the trigger is a button (the press of `usePressCore`, role, tab stop) with `aria-haspopup` from `Type` (`dialog` by default, `menu`, `listbox`, `tree`, `grid`; React Aria omits it for dialogs, ARIA 1.2 allows `dialog`), `aria-expanded` and, while open, `aria-controls` the overlay's ref; `OverlayProps` holds just that ref. `UsePopover(PopoverOptions{Trigger, Open, OnClose, Placement, Offset, Floating, Modal, KeyboardDismissDisabled, ShouldCloseOnInteractOutside, Ref})` returns `{Props, ArrowProps, Placement, Side, Align, Ref}`: `UseFloating(Trigger, Placement (bottom), Offset, Flip, Shift(12), Floating..., Arrow)` and the overlay (always `Dismissable`, with `ShouldCloseOnBlur`); a press outside a non-modal popover does not close it when it is over the trigger's bounds (the trigger's press toggles it; `OnInteractOutside` has no target element to compare, so the bounds decide), and the focus moving to the trigger does not close it; a modal popover closes for the trigger's presses (the trigger is inert, so it is outside). Popovers are non-modal by default (React Aria: modal unless `isNonModal`; the zero value of `Modal` is false). `UseMenuTrigger(MenuTriggerOptions{Trigger ("press", "longPress"), Type, Open..., Disabled, OnPress, Ref, MenuRef})` returns `{TriggerProps, MenuProps, Open, FocusStrategy, SetOpen, Toggle, Close, Pressed, Ref, MenuRef}`: a mouse or a pen opens the menu at the press start and toggles it when open (the non-modal case; a modal menu's trigger is inert), a touch at the release, a virtual click with `FocusFirst`; Enter, Space and ArrowDown toggle it with `FocusFirst`, ArrowUp with `FocusLast` (constants `"first"` and `"last"`; `""` for the menu itself); `longPress` uses `h.OnLongPress` (500 ms), opens with `FocusFirst`, leaves the quick press to `OnPress` (not run for the release of a long press) and needs Alt for the arrow keys; `MenuProps` are the ref, `aria-labelledby` the trigger and a focus scope with `Restore`, for T8's `UseMenu` (which reads `FocusStrategy`, calls `Close` when an item is chosen, and adds the `menu` role, items and keys; `Direction` is not offered). `UseTooltipTrigger(TooltipTriggerOptions{Delay, CloseDelay, Disabled, Trigger ("", "focus"), Open..., Ref, TooltipRef})` returns `{TriggerProps, TooltipProps, Open, Ref, TooltipRef}` (React Aria's `useTooltipTrigger`, `useTooltip` and `useTooltipTriggerState`; there is no separate `UseTooltip`: the tooltip element is rendered only while open, so its props, the `tooltip` role, the ref, hover that keeps it open and Escape, come from the trigger's hook): the pointer must rest `Delay` (1.5 s) on the trigger, a keyboard focus (`core.FocusVisible`) opens at once, `CloseDelay` (0.5 s) closes after leaving, a pointer down or any key down on the trigger and a blur close at once, hover on the tooltip itself keeps it open; the tooltips of a window are a group (`core.TreeLocal`: React Aria's module state, per Tree): opening or warming up one closes the others, and while one is open or for `max(0.5 s, CloseDelay)` after the last closed (`coolAt` on the frame clock) the next opens at once; a zero delay is the default and `aria.NoDelay` (negative) is none. The trigger is `aria-describedby` the tooltip while open (new `h.AriaDescribedBy(ref)`, `core.AriaDescribedBy`: the node's `Description` is the text of the referenced element, `AriaDescription` wins; the snapshot does not print descriptions, as Playwright's). **Engine.** (a) `core.UseTimeout()` is a one-shot timer on the frame clock (an `intervalSlot` with `once`, in `Tree.intervals`, so `NextDeadline`, `TickGestures` and telatest's `Advance` drive it): `Start(d, f)` from handlers at the event's time, `Stop`, `Pending`; no goroutine, and an idle window with a pending tooltip asks for no frame; `core.Clock(ref)` reads that clock. (b) `core.TreeLocal(key, init)` is a value per Tree. (c) `OverlayOptions.OnFocusOutside` (`Tree.focusMoved`, called by `setFocus` once the focus is set, for the overlays that contain the old element and not the new one; a focus that goes nowhere does not call it) and `OverlayOptions.EscapeOnly`: a tooltip is on the stack for Escape (`topEscapeOverlay`: it takes the key before the overlay below, as React Aria's tooltip stops it) but not for presses (`topOverlay` skips it), so a tooltip over a popover does not hide outside presses from it. **Deviations from React Aria**: one hook for modal and non-modal overlays and no underlay props; `ShouldCloseOnInteractOutside` takes the press, not the element; blur closes without asking; popovers non-modal by default; the trigger excluded by bounds; `aria-haspopup="dialog"`; the menu trigger toggles on a mouse press when open; a tooltip has no `getInteractionModality` check (Tela does not emulate hover from touch); the trigger of a tooltip closes on any key down, as React Aria's `onKeyDown` does; `Delay` and `CloseDelay` use zero for the default. | Owner's shapes (options and results with `Props`, state integrated, `*bool` for controlled, `Click`, `Type`). Every behavior is checked in telatest: a modal dialog (the page is out of the snapshot, Tab stays inside, Escape closes, the focus returns to the trigger, an outside press closes a dismissable one and is not delivered to the page, an alert dialog ignores outside presses and the inert page is not clicked), a popover (placed in its first frame under `NoSettle`, closed by Escape, a press outside and the trigger's second press, a press inside a popover opened from it in another Portal does not close it, a press in the outer one closes only the inner, Escape closes the top one only, blur, modal, an exit animation in a `Presence`), a menu trigger (ArrowDown, ArrowUp, Enter, Space with the focus returning, the mouse, a long press and its release, Alt arrows), tooltips (delay, close delay, warm-up and cool-down between two triggers, focus-only, keyboard focus, Escape, press, hover on the tooltip, disabled, controlled, no frames while pending or open), `UseButton{Type}` (submit and reset from a Div with the mouse and Enter), `ElementRef.Click` (from an effect and a handler, a Label, disabled, unmounted), the two-refs panic and its dev-only scope, toggles with `*bool`; core tests for the timeout, `TreeLocal`, the description and the panic. Every golden is bit-identical. Known gaps: no underlay for modal overlays, no `Direction` for the menu trigger, an overlay's `Open` must be passed to `UseOverlay` and `UsePopover` (a component that exists only while open passes `true`) |
| 102 | 2026-10-21 | Icon generator and the Lucide module (phase 8, T6; E2, E3 and the one-variable-per-icon shape are the owner's; amends §5, §17 and ADR-098). **Generator.** `tela icons gen -pkg <name> [-o outdir] [-r] [-prefix text] [-suffix text] [-skip-invalid] [-minify=false] [-source text] [-license name] [-license-file file] [-deprecated file] <dir>` (CLI flags are public API, ADR-049) reads the `*.svg` files of `<dir>` (case-insensitive extension, dot files ignored, subdirectories with `-r`) through `internal/icongen` (the engine; `cmd/tela` stays a thin command, needs no `go` command or module) and writes `outdir/icons_gen.go`: a `// Code generated by tela icons gen; DO NOT EDIT.` header, the `Source:`/`License:` lines and the license text from the flags, a package documentation (count, source, license, an `h.Icon` example, the note that there is no table), `import "github.com/kuronosu/tela"`, and for each icon, sorted by variable name, a doc comment (`// ArrowUp is the icon "arrow-up".`) and `var ArrowUp = tela.Icon{SVG: "…"}` (a raw string literal; an interpreted one when the text has a backtick, a carriage return, a NUL or a BOM), through `go/format`; the output is the same on every run (golden test, `internal/icongen/testdata/demo_icons.go.txt`) and nothing is written when a file fails. **Deprecated names** (amended, T6b): `-deprecated file.json` takes a JSON object from the name of an icon (its file name without `.svg`, slash separated) to the name of the icon that replaces it (`{"alert-circle": "circle-alert"}`); the variable of each listed icon gets a second paragraph in its documentation, `//\n// Deprecated: use CircleAlert.`, so gopls and staticcheck flag its uses, and the file is still generated. A name that is not an icon of `<dir>`, a replacement that is not one, a name deprecated in favor of itself and a chain (the replacement is deprecated too) are errors listing all of them; the flag is the whole interface (no format other than this object). **Validation:** every file is parsed with `internal/svg.Parse`, the parser behind `tela.ParseSVG` (same errors), not with `tela.ParseSVG` itself, so that the CLI does not link the whole engine and the root package stays out of `cmd/tela`; failures are listed with their paths (50 at most, then a count) and stop the command unless `-skip-invalid` leaves them out with a warning; two files that make one name are always an error (listing all of them: skipping one would hide an icon). **Minifier** (`icongen.Minify`, `encoding/xml` raw tokens, written back with its own escaping): drops the XML declaration, processing instructions, comments and the DOCTYPE; elements and attributes in namespaces other than SVG and XLink (Inkscape, Sodipodi, RDF, Illustrator) and the namespace declarations that nothing uses; `metadata`, `title` and `desc` (Tela reads none of them; icons are `aria-hidden` or labelled by `AriaLabel`) and empty `defs`; `class` (unless the document has a `style` element, whose selectors may use it), `id` unless some attribute or text mentions `#id`, `version`, `baseProfile` and `data-*`; white space between elements, and runs of white space in `d`, `points`, `viewBox`, `transform`, `gradientTransform`, `patternTransform`, `stroke-dasharray`, `preserveAspectRatio`. It keeps the text of `text`, `tspan`, `textPath`, `style` and `script` as written, keeps the attribute order, and returns a document that declares entities (an internal DTD subset) unchanged (trimmed). `width` and `height` stay (the file keeps its intrinsic size for `Img` uses). **Equivalence check:** after minifying, `icongen.Equivalent` parses both documents and compares the intrinsic size, size, viewBox, clipping and currentColor use, and the printed display lists (every number exact) in three boxes (24×24, a fractional offset at 16, a wide one) with four option sets (currentColor black and red, the root overrides of `fill-current`/`stroke-none`, and fixed `fill`, `stroke`, `stroke-[1.5]` and `color`); a difference fails the file (`-minify=false` is the escape hatch). Tests run it on the 35 SVG files of the `internal/svg` Chromium galleries (gradients, clip paths, masks, `use`, style sheets, `switch`, entities) plus the generator's own inputs, and check that the minifier is idempotent. **Names** (`icongen.VarName`): the path without `.svg` is split at every character that is not a letter or digit, each piece is capitalized (`arrow-up` is `ArrowUp`, `volume_2` is `Volume2`, `solid/arrow-up` is `SolidArrowUp`), an underscore keeps apart digits that were apart (`arrow-down-0-1` is `ArrowDown0_1`, `arrow-down-01` is `ArrowDown01`: Lucide has both, as an icon and an alias, and without the rule they collide), a name that does not start with an upper-case letter gets `Icon` in front (`3d-rotate` is `Icon3dRotate`, `日本` is `Icon日本`), `-prefix` (an exported identifier start) and `-suffix` go around; Go keywords cannot occur (names are exported, hence capitalized) and are tested. **The Lucide module.** `icons/lucide` is a separate module, `github.com/kuronosu/tela/icons/lucide`, generated from `lucide-static` 1.61.0 (npm; integrity `sha512-yLAiQB3M…`; 2,171 files, 1,902 distinct drawings: Lucide keeps renamed icons as aliases with the same drawing, and all are generated since an unused variable costs nothing, but the 268 old names, 2,171 minus the 1,903 keys of the package's `icon-nodes.json`, are marked `// Deprecated: use X.` through `-deprecated deprecated.json`; the file, committed in the module, is written from the package by `gen_deprecated.go` (`//go:build ignore`, run first by `go generate`), which reads the old names from the SVG files that are not keys of `icon-nodes.json` and the icon that replaces each from the `export { default as Old, default as New } from './icons/new.mjs'` lines of `dist/esm/lucide-static.mjs`, and fails when one has no replacement; `TestDeprecatedNamesPointAtTheCurrentIcon` checks that every name of the file carries the comment, that it points at a declared variable of the right icon that is not itself deprecated, and that no other variable carries one): `icons_gen.go` (957 KiB with the deprecation comments, SVG data 775 KiB after minifying from 1,029 KiB), `gen.go` (`const Version = "1.61.0"` and the `go:generate` line, which reads the SVG directory from `$LUCIDE_ICONS`; a test checks that the header and `Version` agree, and regenerating from the same package gives an identical file), Lucide's `LICENSE` (ISC with the Feather MIT notice, copied unchanged and into the header of the generated file), `README.md` (usage, versions, regeneration with `npm pack --ignore-scripts`), and tests. Its `go.mod` requires `github.com/kuronosu/tela v0.8.0` (the first release with `tela.Icon`, the phase-8 tag of ADR-049; a placeholder until that tag exists) with `replace github.com/kuronosu/tela => ../..`, which dependents ignore; no `go.work` is committed (the replace is enough for `cd icons/lucide && go test ./...`, and workspace mode ignores replace directives, which would hide a require that dependents cannot resolve). At the repository root a directory with its own `go.mod` is outside `./...`, so `go build`, `vet`, `test` and `staticcheck ./...` skip it and nothing in the root module imports it (the module may import `tela`, `h` and `telatest`, never `internal/`). The package has one exported variable per icon and no table by name (the owner's measurement: the linker drops the unused variables, a map links all of them, +0.9 MB for 1,600 icons). **Versioning:** the module's tags mirror Lucide's release: `icons/lucide/vX.Y.Z` holds the icons of Lucide `X.Y.Z` (Go's tag scheme for a module in a subdirectory); agents never create or push them (ADR-049). A regeneration without a new Lucide release (a change of the generator or of the comments only) is published as the pre-release `icons/lucide/v1.61.0-tela.N` (`N` from 1), which Go orders before `v1.61.0` and `@latest` picks only while no release exists, so it is asked for by version; the next Lucide release goes back to `vX.Y.Z` (amended, T6b). Other sets (Heroicons, Tabler…) are added the same way, with the generator and their own module. **CI:** a job `icons-lucide` runs `go vet` and `go test` there with `CGO_ENABLED=0`, and builds it for windows and darwin; the root jobs are unchanged (unverified until pushed). **Conformance** (the phase's criterion, `icons/lucide` `TestChromiumLucide`): the 1,902 distinct icons in a 64-column grid of 32 px cells (2048×960 px), `h.Icon` with `size-[24px]` and `text-[#1e293b]` through `telatest.Mount` at scales 1 and 2 (16 px was measured too, at first; amended, T6b: only 24 px stays in the module), against Chromium 154 showing the same markup inline with the same CSS (`display: block`, a flex-wrapped grid); the screenshots are committed in the module (`testdata/chromium`, 2.0 MB: 24 px at x1 and x2; 3.6 MB with the 16 px ones) with a digest of the icons that makes the test fail when they are old; `TELA_CHROMIUM=1` takes them again (the pages are made in a temporary directory, not committed: 600 KiB each), `TELA_CHROMIUM_DUMP=dir` writes Tela's renderings and the 40 worst cells side by side (Tela, Chromium, difference). It lives in the module and uses only public API (`telatest`, `h`), which keeps Lucide out of the root module and its screenshots out of its tests. Measured, mean difference per RGB channel over a cell (average of the 1,902 icons, median, p99, worst; the 16 px numbers were measured once and are no longer in CI): 16 px x1 0.93, 0.91, 1.77, 2.17 (Flower); 16 px x2 0.52, 0.52, 0.94, 1.11 (Bacon); 24 px x1 0.91, 0.72, 2.50, 3.10 (Grape; Goal, Target, Shell, Moon follow); 24 px x2 0.55, 0.49, 1.34, 1.76 (LayoutGridCircles). As a share of the ink (sum of the differences over the reference's darkness): 16 px x1 6.7% on average (worst 21%), x2 3.7% (10.4%); 24 px x1 3.0% (11.7%, Moon), x2 1.8% (5.5%). The largest single-pixel differences are up to 128 levels (Squirrel at 16 px x1; 110 at 24 px), on anti-aliased edges. The worst icons are circles, rings and curved shapes at scale 1 (Skia's coverage of curves against exact area coverage, as in ADR-095), and small details at 16 px; looking at the 40 worst cells at 24 px and scale 1, the differences are faint outlines along curves, and no icon is displaced, thicker or missing. The test bounds, for 24 px, are 1.05 on the average mean, 3.5 on any icon's mean and 16% of the ink (25% while 16 px was checked). **Binary size** (a Tela program with a `Div` and a `Text`, Linux amd64, `CGO_ENABLED=0`): stripped (`-s -w`) 19,873,952 bytes with no icon, the same importing the lucide package without using it and with `lucide.Check`, 19,902,624 bytes (+28,672, 28 KiB) with 50 icons; unstripped 25,036,308, 25,036,252 (import only), 25,036,279 (1 icon) and 25,069,127 (+32,819) bytes. **Docs:** wiki page Icons (using `h.Icon`, Lucide, the generator and its flags, the size note), the CLI page and Home/_Sidebar, `THIRD_PARTY.md` (Lucide ISC and Feather MIT, redistributed as data), CHANGELOG. **Not done / for later:** `tela icons gen` writes one file (no splitting), the sources of a set are not committed (npm integrity is recorded), no by-name lookup (a user builds a map of the icons they use), and the CI job is unverified until pushed. | The owner's decisions: Lucide separate from the core module, a public generator, one static variable per icon. A nested module keeps the core free of 957 KiB of icon data and of Lucide's releases; the generator being a `tela` command lets anyone build the same set from Heroicons, Tabler or their own SVGs. Minifying with an equivalence check, and not by regex, keeps the data small without a risk of changing a drawing: none of Lucide's 2,171 files needed the escape hatch, and what cannot be proved equal is rejected. Comparing every icon, not a sample, was possible because the whole set renders in about a second per size and scale; it shows the renderer is within about one level of Chromium on average and three at worst for an icon set, the phase's criterion |
| 103 | 2026-10-21 | Gradient-mask classes follow the class order (phase 8, T4b; the owner's decision; amends ADR-098). Mask classes that set the same Tailwind variable follow §10: the class that comes last in the element's class order wins, variants included (a matching `hover:` class later in the string beats an earlier one and loses to a later plain class), not the one Tailwind emits later in its stylesheet. Each family variable takes the form of its last setter: `--tw-mask-linear` is the four edges (`mask-t/r/b/l/x/y-from/to-*`), the angle form `linear-gradient(var(--tw-mask-linear-stops, <angle>))` (`mask-linear-<angle>`, `mask-linear-[…]`) or the stops form (`mask-linear-from/to-*`); `--tw-mask-radial` the size form (`mask-radial-[size]`) or the stops form; `--tw-mask-conic` the angle form or the stops form. The stops variables are the same whichever from/to class sets them, so an angle or size form is valid when any from/to class of its family is present anywhere in the string, and the angle, size, shape, position and every stop color and position are plain variables where the last class wins. So `mask-linear-from-20% mask-b-from-50%` is the edge mask and `mask-b-from-50% mask-linear-from-20%` the linear one; `mask-b-from-50% mask-linear-45` is invalid (an angle without stops: no mask) and `mask-linear-45 mask-b-from-50%` the edge mask. Unchanged from ADR-098: a family that is invalid at computed-value time leaves the element unmasked, the families are intersected, and an explicit `mask-add`/`subtract`/`intersect`/`exclude` wins over the gradients' intersect in either order. No Chromium cell sets one variable twice, so every cell and golden is unchanged | One rule for every class is easier to predict and to explain than Tailwind's stylesheet order, which no one can see from the class string. In practice the change is where the edges and a linear gradient are mixed (ADR-098 let the linear one win in any order); every other variable was already applied in class order. The explicit composite keeps winning because the gradient classes set `mask-composite` as a side effect, not as a choice the author wrote |
| 104 | 2026-10-21 | Collection hooks of package `aria`: `UseListBox`, `UseMenu`, `UseTabs` and `aria.Selection` (phase 8, T8a; the shapes follow the owner's decisions on keyed collections and on the selection type, the names the owner has not seen are listed for review; amends §14, ADR-100 and ADR-101). **Keyed collections.** `UseListBox[K comparable](ListBoxOptions[K])`, `UseMenu[K](MenuOptions[K])` and `UseTabs[K](TabsOptions[K])` take `Keys []K` (the items in display order, given on every build), `DisabledKeys []K` and `TextValue func(K) string` (typeahead), and return a result that embeds `Collection[K]` (`ItemProps(k) tela.Prop`, `ItemRef(k)`, `IsSelected`, `IsDisabled`, `IsFocused`, `IsFocusVisible`, `Focused() (K, bool)`, `FocusKey`, `Focus(strategy)`, `ClearFocus`, `HandleKeyDown`, `ActiveDescendant`, `Section(name)`) next to `Props`, `Selected` and `Ref`; `TabsResult` adds `TabProps` and `PanelProps(k)`. A hook can not be called once per item, so the state is one `collState` per hook call (made with `UseOnce`, read and written directly by the handlers, so a second event before the next build sees the first, as `boolState` does; a counter in `UseState` asks for the build), and every key has an `ElementRef` made by the new `core.NewElementRef()` (a ref that belongs to no hook slot; the engine already detaches it when its element unmounts), which `ItemProps` attaches: that is how a key becomes a DOM focus, a scroll, a bounds query (page keys), `aria-activedescendant`, `aria-controls` and `aria-labelledby`. The refs of keys that leave `Keys` are dropped. An item element holds one ref (ADR-101): `ItemRef(k)` is the one to share. **`aria.Selection[K comparable]`** (the owner's decision; the exact shape is listed for review) is an immutable set, `struct{ s *selectionSet[K] }`: the zero value (`s == nil`) is "not given", which makes an option uncontrolled, and `SelectionOf[K](keys...)` is a value even when empty (a controlled, empty selection); `All[K]()` is React Aria's `"all"`. Methods never change the receiver: `Has`, `With`, `Without`, `Toggle`, `Len`, `Keys` (insertion order, a copy), `In(order)` (the keys in a collection's order: "iteration in key order"), `First()`, `Resolve(order)` (All to its keys), `IsAll`, `IsEmpty`, `IsSet`, `Equal` (by keys, whatever the order; an All and the set of all keys are not equal), `String`. It is comparable with `==` (identity: two equal sets are not `==`, `Equal` compares keys) and usable in `tela.UseState` and as a map key. `Len` and `Keys` of All are 0 and nil (it does not list keys) and `Without` on All returns it unchanged (resolve first). **Controlled or not** follows ADR-101: a non-zero `Selected` is controlled (the list shows it and `OnSelectionChange(Selection[K])` only asks; a selection the app does not store is refused), `DefaultSelected` starts an uncontrolled one, the callback is also called for an uncontrolled list, and an equal selection is not reported. Tabs take the same type (the selected tab is `Selected.First()`); no `SelectedKey *K` was added. Modes are strings, as in the other options of the package (`SelectionMode` `"none"`, `"single"`, `"multiple"`; `SelectionBehavior` `"toggle"`, `"replace"`; `Orientation`; `Direction` `"ltr"`, `"rtl"`; `KeyboardActivation` `"automatic"`, `"manual"`; `EscapeKeyBehavior`; `FocusStrategy`): an unknown one panics in dev mode with the closest valid one. **Rules (React Aria's `useSelectableCollection`, `useSelectableItem`, `ListKeyboardDelegate`, `useTypeSelect` and `SelectionManager`).** ArrowDown and ArrowUp (ArrowLeft and ArrowRight for `Orientation: "horizontal"`, swapped by `Direction: "rtl"`), Home, End, PageUp and PageDown (a viewport away: the item count is the container's height over the stride of the items that are rendered, so keys of a `h.VirtualList` that are not rendered count), `ShouldFocusWrap`, disabled keys skipped, typeahead (case-insensitive prefix, starting at the focused key and then from the top; the 1 s buffer is `core.UseTimeout` on the frame clock, no goroutine; a space inside a search is a character), Shift+arrows and Shift+click extend from the anchor, Ctrl (Cmd on a Mac) +A selects `All` (unless `DisallowSelectAll`), Ctrl+click and Ctrl+Space toggle, Ctrl+arrows move without selecting, with `SelectionBehavior: "replace"` moving the focus moves the selection, Escape clears the selection (`EscapeKeyBehavior`), touch and virtual presses toggle (they have no modifier keys). `OnAction` follows React Aria's primary and secondary actions: with `"toggle"` it runs when the selection is empty, with `"replace"` a click selects and a double click or Enter runs it. A press is a pointer down (it records the pointer type; with a virtual focus it sets the highlight) plus a click, so touch, pen, the semantics click and `ElementRef.Click` are all presses and selection happens on release, as React Aria's `onPress`; Enter and Space are handled by the collection's key handler on the focused key at the key down (not repeats), which stops them. Items are not Buttons (a Button's own Enter and Space click would act twice): use `Div` or `Li`. **Focus.** A roving focus by default: the focused item's element has the focus and `TabIndex(0)`, the others -1, a disabled one none, and the collection is a tab stop (`TabIndex(0)`) only while no key is focused; when it takes the focus (Tab, or a press on it or on a disabled item) the focus goes to the focused item, else to the first selected one, else to the first. Moving the focus focuses the element, which the engine scrolls into view; a key whose item is not rendered (`h.VirtualList`) becomes `pending`: the list scrolls to its index (`ElementRef.ScrollToIndex` through the `Ref` that `Props` carries, so `Props` go to the `VirtualList`) and `ItemProps` gives that item `h.AutoFocus(true)`, so it takes the focus when it mounts. If the focused key leaves `Keys` the focus moves to the item that takes its place (react-stately's `useListState`). `AutoFocus` and `FocusStrategy` (`FocusFirst`, `FocusLast`, from `UseMenuTrigger`; a strategy implies `AutoFocus`) focus on mount: the first selected item, else the strategy's, else the collection itself; it applies when the hook mounts, so the component that calls `UseMenu` is rendered only while the menu is open (a menu that stays mounted when closed does not focus again). **Virtual focus.** `ShouldUseVirtualFocus`: the collection is the tab stop and keeps the DOM focus, the items are not focusable, the focused key is highlighted (`IsFocused`) and `aria-activedescendant` points to its ref, scrolled into view with `ScrollIntoView` (or `ScrollToIndex`). `ExternalFocus` (an addition for T8b's combobox, listed for review) is that for a collection that never has the focus: no tab stop, no handlers on `Props`, a press on an item does not move the focus (its pointer down is prevented), and the element that has the focus calls `HandleKeyDown` from its key handler and carries `ActiveDescendant()`; the keys of a text field stay its own (it handles the arrows, PageUp, PageDown and Enter: no Home, End, Space, Ctrl+A, Escape or typeahead). **Menu.** `UseMenu` adds the `menu` role and items that are `menuitem`, `menuitemradio` (`SelectionMode: "single"`) or `menuitemcheckbox` (`"multiple"`) with `aria-checked`. It adds no focus scope: the `MenuProps` of `UseMenuTrigger` hold the one with `Restore` and the label from the trigger, and `Ref: mt.MenuRef` is shared. The mouse focuses the item it moves over; choosing an item (click, Enter, Space) selects it as the mode says, calls `OnAction(key)` and then `OnClose` (the trigger's `Close`) unless `CloseOnSelect` (a `*bool`; nil closes unless the selection is multiple) says not to. A single selection never unchecks by choosing the checked item (`DisallowEmptySelection`; React Aria's menu would). **Tabs.** `UseTabs` is `useTabList`, `useTab` and `useTabPanel` in one hook: `tablist` with `aria-orientation`; `tab` with `aria-selected` and, while selected, `aria-controls` the panel's ref; `tabpanel` named by its tab (`aria-labelledby`) with `TabIndex(0)`. Only the selected tab is a tab stop (the list has none); automatic activation selects on focus (`SelectionBehavior: "replace"`), `"manual"` selects with Enter and Space; the arrows wrap; there is always one selected, enabled tab (the first when none is given or the given one is disabled or gone; a controlled value that does not hold is shown fixed, without calling the app). **Sections.** `Section(name)` returns `{Props, HeadingProps}`: a `group` named by its heading (`presentation`); the keys stay one flat list. **Engine (small, tested).** (1) `core.NewElementRef()`. (2) `UseMenuTrigger` focuses its trigger at the press start of a mouse or a pen before it opens the menu (React Aria's `usePress` does): the menu's `Restore` scope remembers the focus when it mounts, which is before the engine focuses the pressed button, so after a mouse open, Escape or choosing an item gave the focus back to nothing. Every golden is bit-identical. | The owner's shapes (keyed collections, `Selection` as an immutable set with "all", controlled when non-nil, options in and results with `Props` out). One state per hook with a ref per key is the smallest design that keeps `ItemProps(k)` for any number of items: no hook per item and no new engine concept besides a free ref, and the same machinery serves a `VirtualList`. A pending key plus `AutoFocus` on the item that mounts reuses the focus work of the frame, where an effect would have to run after the builds of the list, which a component's effect does not see. Selection on release and the pointer type taken at the pointer down give React Aria's rules with one handler pair per item. The oracle for the printed roles and states is Playwright 1.61.1 in Chromium, with the checker of ADR-096 (not part of the repository): seven trees (a single and a multiple list box, sections, a menu with a disabled item, radio and checkbox menu items, a tab list with its panel) print byte for byte as Tela prints them (`listbox`, `option [selected]`, `option [disabled]`, `group`, `menu`, `menuitem`, `menuitemradio [checked]`, `menuitemcheckbox [checked]`, `tablist`, `tab [selected]`, `tabpanel`). Telatest covers keyboard navigation (Tab in, Home, End, wrap, disabled keys, typeahead and its expiry on the frame clock, PageUp and PageDown, in a `VirtualList` of 1,000 rows too, where typeahead and End focus rows that are not rendered), single and multiple selection, toggle and replace, Shift ranges with keys and with Shift+click, Ctrl+click, Ctrl+A with a disabled key, controlled (and refused) and uncontrolled, actions and the double click, touch toggling, roving and virtual focus, an external focus driven by an input, the removal of the focused key, auto focus, horizontal and right-to-left lists, sections, menus opened with ArrowDown (first), ArrowUp (last), Enter, Space and the mouse, an action that closes and restores the focus, Escape, hover focus, disabled items and typeahead in a menu, checkbox and radio items, `CloseOnSelect`, tabs with automatic and manual activation, orientation, direction, disabled tabs, the panel link and a controlled selection. | 107 | 2026-10-21 | Phase-8 review fixes, core, and two owner-approved follow-ups (amends ADR-069, ADR-086, ADR-096, ADR-098, ADR-100, ADR-101 and ADR-106). **Implicit submission (ADR-069, ADR-106).** The Form's default button is its first submit button in tree order (`submitButton`): a `Button` of type submit, or any element that a press hook made a button of type submit (`core.DefaultButtonType`, or an explicit `Type` over it), so a `Div` made by `aria.UseButton{Type: "submit"}` is clicked by Enter in any field of a multi-field form (ADR-106 said a Div was not found). The gestures still see the moves and the release (the pan scrolls and flings). The pressed elements stop being `active:` when the pan wins, not at the release, as Chromium clears `:active` when a touch scroll begins. **`SrcFS` cache key (ADR-098).** "When the file system is comparable" becomes "when its value is hashable": `hashable` walks the dynamic value (interfaces, struct fields, array elements; maps, slices and functions are not hashable; pointers are not followed) without `recover`, because a comparable struct whose `fs.FS` field holds an `fstest.MapFS` panicked in the cache's map, under its mutex, and the next load deadlocked. | 109 | 2026-10-21 | Second phase-8 review, core (amends ADR-096, ADR-098 and ADR-107). **Float retry frames per element (ADR-107).** The frames that limits left by a spent relayout budget ask for are counted per floating element (`floatState.retries`, up to `maxFloatRetryFrames`), not per tree: an element's count starts again in a frame that places it without dropping limits, so one whose limits never settle spends its own frames and no longer keeps another from getting its frames (the tree's count was reset only by a frame where no element dropped limits). | 112 | 2026-10-22 | Remote backend, `internal/platform/remote` (phase 9, T1; implements G1a/G2a of ADR-111; amends §15's backend table). **Protocol** ("remote app" protocol, version 1): length-prefixed frames with a hand-written little-endian encoding over an AF_UNIX stream socket (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` or the temporary directory, Windows included), opened by a handshake (hello: magic, version, pid, tag; welcome: host clock, system settings, backend name; or a rejection with its reason). Each side writes from a goroutine fed by a queue, so the host's UI thread never blocks on a child. Window operations carry window IDs (1, 2… in creation order), and a swap maps the new child's window N onto real window N: title, cursor and text input state are applied; size, minimum size and fixed flag stay those of the real window (`platform.Window` cannot change them). The host checks the size before mapping. **Buffering:** two full-frame slots per segment. Present copies only the damage into a free slot and sends slot, generation, metrics and damage; the host's reader goroutine copies the same damage out into its retained frame, under the lock its UI thread presents from, and releases the slot. So the child never writes a slot being read, the host never shows a torn frame, and a slot needs valid pixels only inside its own damage (no buffer-age repair as in the Wayland swapchain); Present waits only when both slots are busy (an error after 5 s). A size change makes a new generation; old segments, and a closed window's unacknowledged one, are unmapped once the host acknowledges a newer generation (Windows needs the name alive until the host opens it). **Host:** `remote.Host` is the `platform.Handler` of any backend; its goroutines (accept, reader and writer per child) hand work to the UI thread with `Wake`, and `Post` runs supervisor code there. Children are active (shown, receive input), pending (receive Configure with the real metrics, FrameEvents on request, focus and settings; render offscreen, their frames kept) or detached; the first child to connect while none is active and input is not frozen becomes active. `Freeze` queues input (pointer, scroll, key, IME, IME session, focus, close requests); `Promote` presents the pending child's latest frame in full, applies its window state, restarts the IME session, sends a pointer enter where the pointer is, replays the queue in order and detaches the old child (the caller stops it); `Unfreeze` replays to the active child. Each child window has a view of its window (focus, held keys, IME session) that every event passes through, so a child that missed events while pending still gets a contract-valid stream (no release without its press). **Forced scale** (`--scale`): ConfigureEvents carry the forced scale with the real logical size; frames are resampled to the real physical size with nearest-neighbor sampling and their damage mapped exactly; a scale change of the real window re-presents without a new child frame. **Overlay hook:** `SetOverlay(func(w, fb))` draws over each composed frame right before Present (every present is then full); a detached or disconnected child's last frame stays on its window, and `Frame` returns a copy of it. **Control channel:** requests (kind, payload, one reply or error) and notifications in both directions, handlers on each side's UI thread; this package gives kinds no meaning. **Measured** (Core Ultra 5 125H, capped to 4 CPUs): a full-frame Present (child copy and host copy, until the slot is released) costs 2.3 ms at 1920×1080 and 6.3 ms at 2880×1800, 3.2 ms and 7.9 ms until the real (headless) window presented it; an input event takes 51 µs from the host window to the child's handler; a 2× nearest-neighbor upscale of 1440×900 takes 5.4 ms | Copying out on the host's reader goroutine keeps the slots independent of how busy the host's UI thread is, so Present costs two memcpys of the damage and almost never waits; damage-only slots make two slots enough without the swapchain's repair logic. Descriptor passing needs no names or cleanup on Unix; Windows has no equivalent over AF_UNIX, and DuplicateHandle would need a handle to the host process. Children run with `TELA_REMOTE`, `TELA_REMOTE_TAG` (the build generation), `TELA_DEVLOOP=1`, `TELA_DEVLOOP_NO_STATE=1` with `--no-state`, and `TELA_DEV=1` unless the user sets `TELA_DEV`; their stdout/stderr are forwarded, stdin is the null device, and they run in their own process group (Unix `Setpgid`, Windows `CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP`) so Ctrl+C reaches only tela dev, which stops them. **Swap.** After a good build, if a child is alive: `Freeze`; `state.save` to the active child (1 s; an error or no handler proceeds without state and says why); start the new child, answering its `state.load` with the bytes (empty for none); wait for its first frame (15 s); `Promote`; stop the old one (`tela.dev.quit`, then after 1 s close the connection and kill). A new child that exits, panics or times out before its first frame is killed, input is unfrozen and the old child stays. With no child alive (a runtime crash) the next good build starts fresh: no state, no freeze. Builds that finish during a swap wait for it; only the latest is swapped in. An exit is classified once the child's connection has ended (or 500 ms after the exit if it never connected), because its last control messages (a panic report) reach the supervisor through the host's UI thread, unordered with the process's exit. The active child exiting with status 0 (its window closed) quits tela dev, and so does a close request while no child is alive, `q`, SIGINT/SIGTERM/SIGHUP (a second one kills the children at once) and the end of the backend's loop (status 1). **Control kinds** (`internal/devloop`, standard library only, imported by `cmd/tela` and by `teladev` files): `tela.dev.state.save` (host→child request, reply opaque bytes), `tela.dev.state.load` (child→host request before mounting, reply the bytes or empty), `tela.dev.state.report` (child→host notification, two uvarints kept/reset, printed as "state: 12 kept, 1 reset"), `tela.dev.panic` (child→host notification, "panic: <value>\n\n<stack>", sent before a non-zero exit; the terminal gets one summary line since the child prints the trace on stderr), `tela.dev.assets` (child→host notification, NUL-separated absolute `os.DirFS` roots, the full list each time), `tela.dev.quit` (host→child notification: exit 0 promptly). **Flags.** `--size` rewrites the options of the real window (`HostOptions.WindowOptions`, new in `remote`), `--dark` forces `DarkMode` in the settings children see (`HostOptions.Settings`, new; a system change it hides is not forwarded) so `App.ColorScheme` still wins, `--scale` is `HostOptions.Scale`, `-v` prints each step and the timing of each reload. **Terminal** (`cmd/tela/internal/term`): when stdin is a terminal, cbreak mode (termios without `ICANON`/`ECHO`, keeping `ISIG` and output processing, so Ctrl+C still signals and children's `\n` still returns the carriage; console mode without line input and echo on Windows); `r` reload, `R` reload with fresh state, `q` quit; restored on every exit path, including panics on the supervisor's goroutines (each restores it before re-panicking). One line per event: `building…`, `started in …`, `reloaded in 280ms (state: 1 kept, 0 reset)`, `restarted in …` after a crash, `build failed:` and the compiler output, `the app panicked: …`. **Errors.** Problems go through one seam, `problemView{show(problem), clear()}` (kinds build, panic, exit, start; a one-line summary and the full text), printed by the terminal view; T5 adds the overlay over the last good frame there (`Host.SetOverlay`). **Measured** (Core Ultra 5 125H): with fake children (the test binary speaking the protocol) a reload takes 15 ms from the key to the promotion; with a real `go build` of the 84-line `cmd/tela/testdata/devfake` (warm cache, headless) a save reloads in 276–328 ms: 100 ms debounce, 172–224 ms build, 0.15 ms state save, 2.7 ms start to first frame, 0.05 ms promotion; on Wayland at `--scale 2` the first frame took 20 ms from the start and its promotion 5 ms. The end-to-end test on a real Tela app came with the child runtime (ADR-114) | One event loop with no locks around its state makes the swap's many asynchronous steps (build, save, process start, connection, first frame, exit) easy to reason about and to test with fake children; the host stays responsive because nothing waits on its thread. Process groups keep a terminal Ctrl+C from killing a child mid-swap. Filtering before the debounce, and watching only modules built from source, keep app logs and the module cache from triggering builds. The `init` runner is the only way to run a preview of a package main without copying it; the overlay keeps the source tree clean |
