A child that dies without Close can still leave the host one failed open, which stays a non-fatal host error (`-v` only in `tela dev`). The event-encoding test skips its comparison with package `platform`'s sources when the test binary runs outside the source tree (the round trips still run). **Removed names on Windows** (ADR-115): `ReadDirectoryChangesW`'s records do not say whether a removed or renamed-away name was a directory, and the watcher reported every extensionless name as a possible one, which a filter must accept: vim's `4913` probe passed a filter that accepts directories, and any extensionless file removed in the module (`LICENSE`, `Makefile`) rebuilt the app. Each root now keeps the set of its tree's directories (lowercased; walked while the first read is pending, so a directory created meanwhile is walked or reported, and again after an overflow; updated by the records), and only names in it are reported as directories. **A killed child** on Windows exits with status 1 (`Process.Kill` is `TerminateProcess(1)`; there is no signal), which the problem line reports as such; a test now expects the OS's wording. **Defender:** Microsoft Defender scans every new executable on its first run, and every reload is a new build: about 690 ms for a 29 MB build (12 ms on its second run); a reload of `examples/dashboard` took 2.2 s, and 1.1 s with the temporary directory and the Go build cache excluded (build 1.4 → 0.8 s, start → first frame 0.7 → 0.18 s). `tela dev` cannot avoid the scan and never changes the system's settings. On Windows, when a build takes more than 400 ms from its start to its first frame (90–180 ms without the scan), it prints once per session the likely cause, the directories to exclude from real-time scanning and the PowerShell command to run as administrator (`Add-MpPreference -ExclusionPath '…', '…'`); the note is also in `tela dev --help` and the CLI wiki page. So that a few directories cover every session and all of a build's files, Windows builds live in `%TEMP%\tela-dev\session-*` (the hint names `%TEMP%\tela-dev`; elsewhere sessions stay `tela-dev-*` in the temporary directory, since a fixed directory in a shared `/tmp` would belong to its first user) and the go command's temporary files (its `WORK` directory, where it compiles and links before copying the executable out) go to the session's directory through `GOTMPDIR`, unless the user set `GOTMPDIR`, which the hint then names too; `GOCACHE` and `GOTMPDIR` come from the `go env -json` that `tela dev` already runs (ADR-123). The threshold and the exclusions are supervisor settings, so the fake-child tests check the hint on every OS | Releasing a segment before the host acknowledged it was already wrong for a closed window (ADR-112 kept it); Close is the same case. Knowing the tree's directories is what inotify gets from `IN_ISDIR`, and a guess from the name cannot tell `4913` from a package directory. A scan the user did not ask for cannot be skipped by `tela dev`, and changing antivirus settings is the user's decision, so it explains once what the slow start is and how to avoid it, with one directory that covers every session instead of a new random one each time |

| 127 | 2026-10-22 | Windows console window (the owner's decision). Go builds Windows executables for the console subsystem by default, so Windows opens a console window next to a Tela app started from Explorer, a shortcut or the Start menu. **Release builds use `-ldflags=-H=windowsgui`** (GUI subsystem: only the app's window opens), documented in Getting Started ("Ship it") and the README; phase 17's `tela build` sets it for Windows targets by default and, since a GUI program has no standard output or error, writes panics and the app's log to a file in its data directory. **Tela does not hide or detach the console at run time** (`FreeConsole`/`ShowWindow` when the process owns its console): the console would still flash on screen at every start, which users read as malware. Development is unaffected: `go run` and `tela dev` run from a terminal and use it | A console flash at start looks like a virus; the subsystem flag is the standard fix (Fyne, Gio, Wails document the same) and costs nothing at run time; output must not be lost silently in shipped apps, hence the log file in `tela build` |
| 128 | 2026-10-25 | `tela new <dir>` creates an app (the owner's request). **Templates** are plain files in `cmd/tela/templates/app`, embedded with `go:embed` and rendered with `text/template` (a `.tmpl` suffix keeps the go command from reading `go.mod` and `.go` files there). **What it writes:** `go.mod` (module path from `-module` or the directory name, `go` directive equal to Tela's own, checked by a test), `main.go` (the Counter of Getting Started, a public `Counter()` wrapper, `var App` so `tela dev`, `snapshot`, `inspect` and the test share one window description), `main_test.go` with `telatest.MountApp`, a README and a `.gitignore`. **Dependencies:** unless `-skip-deps`, it runs `go get github.com/kuronosu/tela@<v>` and `go mod tidy` with `CGO_ENABLED=0`; `<v>` is the binary's own version when it is a release or pseudo-version, `latest` for a build from a checkout, so `go install …@v0.9.1` yields apps on v0.9.1. If that fails the files stay, the exit code is 1 and the message gives the two commands to run. **Safety:** it refuses a directory that is not empty, writes nothing before the checks pass, and has no network use of its own. **Not done:** `tela init` for an existing module, other templates (`tela new --template`), and `tela add` stay deferred. |

A radio is checked by its **click**, not by a press hook: a click is every press (mouse, touch, pen, the semantics click, `ElementRef.Click`, a Label's forwarded click), and a radio needs no `Pressed` state, so there is no per-item `usePressCore` (a hook cannot be called once per key); **no hidden input and no form value** (Tela has no form data for custom controls, ADR-069). **`UseToggleGroup[K comparable](ToggleGroupOptions[K])`** (React Aria's `useToggleButtonGroup`, `useToggleButtonGroupItem` and `useToolbar`): `Keys`, `DisabledKeys`, `Disabled`, `SelectionMode` (`"single"` by default, as React Aria's; `"multiple"`), `Selected`/`DefaultSelected`/`OnSelectionChange` with `aria.Selection[K]` (ADR-104's controlled rule: the zero Selection leaves it to the hook, an equal selection is not reported), `DisallowEmptySelection`, `Orientation` (`"horizontal"`), `Direction`, `ShouldFocusWrap`, `AriaLabel`, `AriaLabelledBy`, `Ref`; the result has `Props`, `ItemProps(k)`, `ItemRef(k)`, `IsSelected(k)`, `Selected` and `Ref`. As in React Aria, `"single"` is a `radiogroup` of `radio` items with `aria-checked` and `"multiple"` a `toolbar` of `button` items with `aria-pressed`; a press on the item that is on turns it off unless `DisallowEmptySelection`. The group is one tab stop that remembers the item that had the focus (else the first item that is on, else the first enabled one), the arrows of the orientation move the focus without selecting (up and down are not the horizontal group's), skipping disabled items; Enter and Space toggle on the key down. **Deviations:** Home and End jump (Radix's toggle group has them, React Aria's toolbar does not); the arrows do not wrap unless `ShouldFocusWrap` (React Aria's toolbar calls `focusNext()` without `wrap`, as far as the port could verify; Radix's `loop` defaults to true, so the kit passes it). **`UseSlider(SliderOptions)`** (React Aria's `useSlider`, `useSliderThumb` and `useSliderState`): `Values *[]float64`, `DefaultValues`, `OnValuesChange([]float64)`, `OnChangeEnd([]float64)` (the callbacks get copies), `Min` (0), `Max` (100 when both are 0; `Max <= Min` panics in dev mode), `Step` (1; a negative one panics), `MinStepsBetweenThumbs`, `Orientation`, `Direction`, `Disabled`, `FormatValue(float64) string`, `AriaLabel`, `AriaLabelledBy`, `ThumbLabels []string`; the result has `Props` (`group` and its name), `TrackProps`, `LabelProps`, `ThumbProps(i)`, `ThumbRef(i)`, `TrackRef`, `ThumbPercent(i)`, `ValueText(i)`, `IsThumbDragging(i)`, and `Values` and `Dragging` of the build. **There is no single-value convenience**: the values are a slice, so a slider with one thumb is `[]float64{v}`, which is Radix's and shadcn's shape; the thumb count is `len(*Values)`, else `len(DefaultValues)`, else one thumb at `Min`. The rules of the state are react-stately's: a value is rounded by `snapValueToStep` (a port, with `roundToStepPrecision`) between its neighbors' values (`+/- MinStepsBetweenThumbs * Step`), so thumbs never cross; a press on the track picks the nearest thumb (the lower one on a tie, the first or the last past the ends), focuses it, moves it to the point and goes on dragging it; a press on a thumb drags it by the pointer's movement (the grab offset is kept: no jump); a mouse is captured by the element that was pressed (`core.SetPointerCapture`) and touch and pen by their implicit capture, so a drag follows the pointer anywhere, and a cancel ends it; keys: the arrows move a thumb by `Step` (a page with Shift), PageUp and PageDown by a page (`max(Step, round(range/10/Step)*Step)`), Home and End to the thumb's limits, `Direction: "rtl"` mirrors left and right, and a vertical slider has `Min` at the bottom; `OnChangeEnd` follows every key that changed a value and every drag, as React Aria's `onChangeEnd` does. A thumb is `slider` with `aria-valuenow`, `aria-valuemin` and `aria-valuemax` (its limits, the neighbors' for a range slider, as React Aria's range input), `aria-valuetext` (`FormatValue`, by default the number as it is written; locale digits are the caller's, with `i18n`, since a default from the system locale would make the output depend on the machine), `aria-orientation`, a tab stop, and a name from `ThumbLabels[i]`, else `AriaLabel`, else the element with `LabelProps`. **Dragging repaints, it does not build:** `ThumbPercent(i)` (0 to 100: React Aria's `getThumbPercent` is a fraction, and the name says percent) reads a `UseValue[int]` version counter and the hook's own state, so it is meant for the paint-phase closure of the thumb (`tela.TranslateFn(func() (x, y float32) { return float32(s.ThumbPercent(i)) / 100 * track.Get().Width, 0 })`, with `track` the `tela.UseSize(s.TrackRef)`); a pointer move of an uncontrolled slider updates the state at once and `core.PublishValue`s the counter (ADR-096: the paint readers are evaluated again in that frame), and the component builds when the drag starts, when it ends and when the pointer has rested for 120 ms (`core.UseTimeout`), so that `aria-valuenow` and the semantics catch up without a build per move. A controlled slider shows what the caller passes (a build publishes the counter when the values changed, so the thumb is right in the same frame) and builds as often as the caller's state changes; a change that the caller refuses leaves the thumb where it was. **Measured** (`TestSliderDragDoesNotBuildPerMove`, 20 moves of one drag): 2 builds (start and end), 2 layouts, a repaint per move, and the thumb is under the pointer after every move. Touch and pen drag like a mouse; `touch-action` is not supported (§10), so a touch drag inside a scroller can become a scroll and be cancelled. **`UseProgressBar(ProgressBarOptions)` and `UseMeter(MeterOptions)`** (React Aria's `useProgressBar` and `useMeter`): `Value`, `Min`, `Max` (as the slider's), `FormatValue`, `AriaLabel`, `AriaLabelledBy` and, for the bar, `Indeterminate` (no `aria-valuenow` or `-valuetext`, `Percent` 0); the result (`ProgressBarResult`) has `Props` (`progressbar` or `meter`, the range and the text), `LabelProps`, `Percent` (0 to 100, again not React Aria's fraction) and `ValueText` (the rounded percentage, `"40%"`, unless `FormatValue`). The meter's role is `meter`, where React Aria writes `"meter progressbar"` (a fallback list that Tela's `Role` does not take). **Aria snapshot** (as Playwright 1.61 prints the equivalent HTML): `radiogroup "Plan"` with `radio "Pro" [checked]`; `radiogroup` or `toolbar` with `radio [checked]` or `button [pressed]` items; `group "Volume"` with `slider "Volume"` (no value: the snapshot prints the values of text fields only); `progressbar "Upload"`; `meter "Disk usage"`. **Names the owner has not seen:** `UseRadioGroup`, `RadioGroupOptions`, `RadioGroupResult`, `RadioProps`, `RadioID`, `RadioRef`, `DefaultValue *K`, `OnValueChange`, `UseToggleGroup`, `ToggleGroupOptions`, `ToggleGroupResult`, `ItemProps`, `ItemRef`, `DisallowEmptySelection`, `ShouldFocusWrap` (also a `ListBoxOptions` name), `UseSlider`, `SliderOptions`, `SliderResult`, `TrackProps`, `TrackRef`, `ThumbProps`, `ThumbRef`, `ThumbPercent`, `ThumbLabels`, `ValueText`, `IsThumbDragging`, `OnValuesChange`, `OnChangeEnd`, `MinStepsBetweenThumbs`, `FormatValue`, `UseProgressBar`, `UseMeter`, `ProgressBarOptions`, `MeterOptions`, `ProgressBarResult`, `Percent`, `Indeterminate` | The kit's radio group, segmented control, slider, progress bar and meter need behavior that is right once (keys, focus, roles, drag, rounding) and a state that the rules of ADR-101 and ADR-104 already describe, so the hooks reuse them (`*K`, `*[]float64`, `Selection`) instead of inventing a state model. A radio is checked by a click and a toggle item toggles on one because a hook is called once per group and the engine's click already is every press. The slider is where a hook can protect the frame budget, since a drag has to follow the pointer at the display rate: reading a Value in a paint closure and publishing it in the frame (ADR-096) makes the drag cost a repaint, which the measurement shows, while the 120 ms rebuild keeps what assistive technology and tests read honest. Keeping React Aria's rounding, neighbor limits and closest-thumb rule means that a range slider behaves as the reference does with every pointer and key. Rendering is unchanged: every golden of the repository is bit-identical |
| 133 | 2026-10-25 | Menu bar and context menu hooks of package `aria` (phase 10, T6; the hooks that the kit's Menubar and ContextMenu are built on, ADR-129; every public shape is listed for the owner's review; amends §14, ADR-101 and ADR-104). **`UseMenuBar[K comparable](MenuBarOptions[K])`** (the WAI-ARIA menubar pattern, with the rules of ADR-101's `UseMenuTrigger` and Radix's Menubar): `Keys`, `DisabledKeys`, `Disabled`, `Open *K` (controlled; a key that is not in `Keys`, such as `""`, means all closed), `DefaultOpen *K`, `OnOpenChange(key K, open bool)` (called with the menu that opens, or the one that closes; moving from one menu to another is one call, with the one that opens), `Direction`, `NoWrap` (the pattern wraps, so the zero value is the default), `AriaLabel`, `AriaLabelledBy`, `Ref`. The result has `Props` (`menubar`, `aria-orientation`, the name), `TriggerProps(k)`, `MenuProps(k)`, `PopoverOptions(k) PopoverOptions`, `MenuRef(k)`, `TriggerRef(k)`, `IsOpen(k)`, `SetOpen(k, open)`, `Open`, `OpenKey`, `FocusStrategy`, `Close` and `Ref`. **One hook for the bar, one menu rendered**: a hook cannot be called per key, and only one menu is open, so the open menu is a component of the caller's (keyed with `tela.WithKey(menu, bar.OpenKey)` so that each mounts anew and focuses its first item) that calls `UsePopover(bar.PopoverOptions(k))` and `UseMenu` with `Ref: bar.MenuRef(k)`, `FocusStrategy: bar.FocusStrategy` and `OnClose: bar.Close`, and puts `bar.MenuProps(k)` on the element (the ref, `aria-labelledby` the trigger, a focus scope with `Restore`, and the keys below). `PopoverOptions(k)` is a value to adjust (`Offset`, `Floating`): `bottom-start`, closed through the bar and **not modal**, because a modal popover makes the other triggers inert and hover could not switch menus; a press over any trigger's bounds is not an outside press, so it is the trigger's own. **Triggers**: `menuitem` + `aria-haspopup="menu"` + `aria-expanded` (+ `aria-controls` while open), a button type that is never a Form's default, a tab stop on the trigger that had the focus last (else the first enabled one: `rover`, ADR-132). Their press is the `presser` of ADR-100 built once per key (`pressState` per key in the hook; `usePressCore` is hooks and could not be called in a loop; `presser.handlers()` is the extracted part): a mouse or a pen opens on the press, a touch on the release, a virtual click with `FocusFirst`, and a press on the open menu's trigger closes it. **Keys** (APG, React Aria's menu trigger): ArrowLeft and ArrowRight move the focus (swapped by `rtl`, wrapping unless `NoWrap`, skipping disabled triggers), Home and End jump; ArrowDown, Enter and Space toggle the menu with `FocusFirst`, ArrowUp with `FocusLast`; with a menu open, ArrowLeft and ArrowRight in the menu (and on its trigger) close it and open the neighbor's on its first item, and hovering another trigger (not a touch) opens that menu with the menu itself focused; Escape and a primary press outside close it and the scope gives the focus back to the open menu's trigger; **Tab** focuses the trigger, closes the menu and leaves the key's default action alone, so the focus goes on from the bar's only tab stop (Shift+Tab backwards). A move first focuses the new trigger with `switching` set, so that the blur of the old menu (its popover's `ShouldCloseOnBlur`) does not close the menu that is about to open; the popover's `OnClose` only closes its own key. **Not included**: submenus (ADR-129), typeahead on the triggers and mnemonics (Alt+letter), both optional in the task. **`UseContextMenuTrigger(ContextMenuTriggerOptions)`** (the contextmenu event and Radix's ContextMenu; React Aria has none): `Open *bool`, `OnOpenChange(bool)` (ADR-101's rule; no `DefaultOpen`, a menu opens at a point), `Disabled`, `NoLongPress`, `Ref`, `MenuRef`; the result is shaped like `MenuTriggerResult` (`TriggerProps`, `MenuProps`, `Open`, `FocusStrategy`, `SetOpen`, `Close`, `Ref`, `MenuRef`) plus `OpenAt(x, y)`, `Anchor func() tela.Rect` (the virtual anchor, `AnchorRect`'s argument) and `PopoverOptions()` (an `AnchorRect(Anchor)` in `Floating`, `bottom-start`, flipped and shifted by `UsePopover`). `TriggerProps` has the ref, `h.OnContextMenu` (a right click: at the pointer, no item focused; prevents the default and stops propagation, so the innermost area wins), `h.OnLongPress` (a touch only, `PointerTouch`: at the finger with `FocusFirst`) and `h.OnKeyDown` (Shift+F10 and the ContextMenu key, not on repeat: `FocusFirst`, at the area's top-left corner); the position is a `tela.UseValue(tela.Rect)` that the handlers set and `Anchor` reads, so the menu is placed in the frame that shows it. `MenuProps` are the ref, a focus scope with `Restore` and a contextmenu handler that stops a right click on the menu from reaching the area (the menu has no `aria-labelledby`: the area is not a label; name it with `MenuOptions.AriaLabel`). The popover is **not modal**: a second right click on the area reaches it and moves the menu (the position Value changes and the float pass places it again), a primary press outside closes it. **Limits that need the engine, reported rather than changed**: (1) `OnInteractOutside` is primary-press only (`outsideDown`), so a right click outside the area leaves the menu open; (2) the long press is recognized for every pointer of the area (`OnLongPress` has no pointer filter), so a mouse button held 500 ms on the area or on a child button does not click (`NoLongPress` turns the gesture off); (3) `KeyboardEvent` has no target and there is no focused-element query, so a keyboard menu opens at the area's corner and not at the focused child. **Names the owner has not seen:** `UseMenuBar`, `MenuBarOptions`, `MenuBarResult`, `NoWrap`, `OpenKey`, `PopoverOptions` (as a method of both results), `MenuRef(k)`, `TriggerRef(k)`, `IsOpen`, `UseContextMenuTrigger`, `ContextMenuTriggerOptions`, `ContextMenuTriggerResult`, `NoLongPress`, `Anchor`, `OpenAt` | The kit's Menubar needs the pattern right once (roving focus, the switch between open menus, Tab, hover), and a context menu needs the pointer as an anchor, which `UseFloating`'s virtual rectangles (ADR-099) were made for. Both reuse `UseMenu`, `UsePopover` and the menu trigger's focus rules, so the menu rendered by a dropdown menu, a menu bar and a context menu is the same component. The bar is one hook with a rover and per-key presses because that is what a Go hook can be, as in ADR-132; the popover is non-modal because hover switching is the pattern, and the same choice lets a context menu be moved by another right click. Rendering is unchanged: every golden of the repository is bit-identical |
| 134 | 2026-10-25 | Calendar hooks of package `aria` and `Formatter.MonthYear` (phase 10, T8; the hooks that the kit's Calendar and date pickers are built on, ADR-129; every public shape is listed for the owner's review; amends §14, ADR-087, ADR-101 and ADR-131). **`aria.Date{Year, Month, Day}`** is the day of the Gregorian calendar, with no time or zone: a comparable value (a `time.Time` is not reliably so: `==` also compares the zone pointer and the monotonic reading), so it is a map key, the state of `UseState` and a prop that skips equal builds, and its arithmetic (`AddDays`, `AddMonths` and `AddYears`, which clamp the day like `java.time` and `@internationalized/date`: January 31 plus a month is February 28 or 29; `Compare`, `Before`, `After`, `Weekday`, `String` as ISO 8601, `IsValid`, `DateOf(time.Time)`, `Time(*time.Location)`, `Today()`) has no daylight saving to trip on; the zero Date is none; `aria.DateRange{Start, End}` is a span with both ends. **`UseCalendar(CalendarOptions)` and `UseRangeCalendar(RangeCalendarOptions)`** (React Aria's `useCalendar`, `useRangeCalendar`, `useCalendarGrid` and `useCalendarCell` with react-stately's `useCalendarState` and `useRangeCalendarState`) return one `CalendarResult`. Options: `Value *Date` (`*DateRange`), `DefaultValue`, `OnValueChange` (ADR-101's rule: nil is uncontrolled, a pointer is controlled and a change the caller does not store is refused, the callback also follows an uncontrolled calendar); `FocusedDate *Date`, `DefaultFocusedDate`, `OnFocusChange` (the same rule for the date with the tab stop, which decides the month on display; it starts at the selected date, else today); `Min`, `Max`, `IsDateUnavailable func(Date) bool`, `Disabled`, `ReadOnly`, `VisibleMonths` (1 to 12), `FirstDayOfWeek *time.Weekday` (nil: `Formatter.FirstDayOfWeek`), `FixedWeeks`, `Today Date` (the injected clock: the zero Date is the wall clock; the frame clock is not wall time), `Locale` and `Direction` (empty: the component's locale and its writing direction), `AriaLabel`, `AriaLabelledBy`, `PreviousLabel` and `NextLabel` (English by default; the hook has no catalog), `Ref`, and for ranges `AllowsNonContiguousRanges`. Result: `Props` (`application`, named `AriaLabel + ", " + Title` as React Aria's), `Title` (the visible range: `MonthYear` of the first month, joined by an en dash to the last one when several are shown), `Value`/`Range`, `FocusedDate`, `Months()`, `Month(i)`, `Heading(i)`, `HeadingProps(i)`, `GridProps(i)` (`grid` named by its heading, `aria-readonly`, `aria-multiselectable` for ranges), `RowProps()`, `WeekdayNames(i18n.NameWidth)` (ordered from the first day), `ColumnHeaderProps(i)` (`columnheader` named by the full weekday), `Weeks(i) [][]Date` (the weeks the month needs, four to six, or six with `FixedWeeks`, with the neighbouring months' dates around it), `DayText(d)`, `CellProps(i, d)`, `PrevProps()`, `NextProps()` (named, `Disabled` at Min and Max), `PrevDisabled()`, `NextDisabled()` and the queries `IsSelected`, `IsInRange`, `IsRangeStart`, `IsRangeEnd`, `Anchor`, `IsToday`, `IsOutsideMonth(i, d)`, `IsUnavailable`, `IsDisabled`, `IsFocused`. **Rules** (React Aria's): arrows by a day or a week (left and right swapped by a right-to-left direction, which defaults to the locale's), PageUp and PageDown by a month and with Shift by a year, the day of the month kept or clamped, Enter and Space select (not on repeat); focus is limited to Min and Max (the focus stops at the limit and a date outside them is `Disabled`); an unavailable date can be focused and has `aria-disabled`, but a press on it only focuses it; the month on display follows the focus (`alignStart`: the window moves to the focused month when it leaves it, limited to the months of Min and Max) and the month buttons move it and the focused date by `VisibleMonths`; a range is two presses (the first anchors, the pointer over a date of the month or the focus on it previews up to it, the second completes it with the earlier date first, Escape cancels), and while it is anchored the dates beyond the nearest unavailable one on each side (searched three years) are unavailable too, unless `AllowsNonContiguousRanges`. **Deviations** (each listed for the owner): (1) a cell is **one** element, a focusable `gridcell` (the ARIA grid pattern's roving gridcell) where React Aria has a `td` with an inner `role=button` `div`, so the kit renders one `Button` per day and `CellProps` takes the month index too (`CellProps(i, d)`: with two months a neighbouring date is a cell of both grids, and a ref belongs to one element); (2) Home and End go to the first and last day of the week (the APG's keys, as the brief asked), where React Aria moves to the start and end of the visible page (as far as the port could verify); (3) the cell's name is the full localized date (`Formatter.Date(Full)`) without React Aria's localized "Today", "Selected" and "Not available" words, which need strings the hook has no catalog for (`aria-selected` and `aria-disabled` carry the state); there is no `aria-current` because `h` has no `AriaCurrent`; (4) dates of the neighbouring months are cells and pressing one moves to its month (React Aria hides them; shadcn shows them), and the focus of such a cell does not move the month until it is pressed; (5) no drag selection of ranges, no live region that announces the month, no `aria-describedby`; (6) the move of the DOM focus to a cell that is not rendered yet is the collections' (ADR-104): `AutoFocus` on the cell that mounts, plus an effect for an element that stands for another date after the build, so the caller keys the cells by `Date.String()` and each grid by its month. **`Formatter.MonthYear(t)`** is CLDR's `yMMMM` available format ("October 2026", "octubre de 2026", "2026年10月", "2026. október", "октябрь 2026 г."), vendored beside the other patterns by the generator (`availableFormats.yMMMM` of each locale file, `-fetch` keeps only that skeleton; the output stays deterministic) and run by the existing LDML interpreter, which now takes `L` (and `c`) as the stand-alone form that CLDR means, where it used the format names: `LLLL y` is "октябрь 2026", not "октября 2026", and the one pattern of the vendored data that uses `c` (`cccc d. MMMM y` of Finnish) now writes "maanantai", not "maanantaina"; no other output changes. **Cost:** the vendored data grows from 506,602 to 514,114 bytes, `dates_gen.go` from 168,131 to 169,340, and an app that imports `aria` but not `i18n` or `kit` now links `i18n` (the hello-world probe, a window with one `UseButton`: 25.25 to 25.60 MB unstripped, 20.02 to 20.26 MB stripped, of which the calendar code and `i18n` are all the growth). **Aria snapshot:** `application "Appointment, February 2026"` with the previous and next `button`s, a `heading`, a `grid "February 2026"` of `row`s of `columnheader`s (named by the weekday) and `gridcell "Tuesday, February 10, 2026" [selected]`, `[disabled]` for a date before Min or unavailable; checked byte for byte against Playwright **1.64.1** (the only one installed in the machine of this session; ADR-131's checker was 1.61.1) for a page with the same roles and attributes, as `aria/testdata/calendar_*.aria.txt` | The kit's Calendar, DatePicker and DateRangePicker need behavior that is right once (the keys, the month that follows the focus, the limits, the anchored range), and a date with no zone is what a calendar selects: a value type is the way to make that comparable and free of daylight saving, and `time.Time` is one conversion away. One cell element per day gives the kit one `Button` to style and the engine half the elements. Keeping React Aria's rules for unavailable dates, limits and ranges means a booking calendar behaves as the reference does with every key and pointer. A stand-alone `L` is what ICU does and the only way for `MonthYear` to say "октябрь" in Russian without a pattern of its own. Rendering is unchanged: every golden of the repository is bit-identical |
| 135 | 2026-10-25 | Color hooks of package `aria` (phase 10, T9; the hooks that the kit's color picker is built on, ADR-129; every public shape is listed for the owner's review; amends §14, ADR-101 and ADR-132). **The color type lives in `aria`**, not on `tela.Color`: `aria.HSB{H, S, B, A}` (degrees 0 to 360, percentages 0 to 100, opacity 0 to 1) and `aria.HSL`, with `ToHSB(tela.Color)`, `ToHSL`, `HSB.Color()`, `HSL.Color()`, `HSB.HSL()`, `HSL.HSB()`, `ParseHexColor(s) (tela.Color, error)` (`#rgb`, `#rgba`, `#rrggbb`, `#rrggbbaa`, any case, the `#` optional, spaces trimmed, an error that names the problem) and `FormatHexColor(c, alpha)` (lower case, 8-bit rounding, clamped). Conversions are plain float64 functions (CSS Color's HSV algorithm) that clamp to the sRGB gamut (extended-sRGB components are clamped first) and give a gray the hue 0; a change to the root package was not needed (`tela.Hex` panics, `ParseHexColor` returns the error a text field must revert on). **`UseColorState(ColorStateOptions{Value *tela.Color, DefaultValue, OnChange(tela.Color)})`** (react-stately's `useColorPickerState`) is the color that the area, the sliders and the field share, ADR-101's rule (nil `Value` is uncontrolled, a pointer controlled and a refused change leaves the color, `OnChange` also follows an uncontrolled state). It keeps the color as an `HSB`, the shown one, the last one asked for and the last build's, so that **the hue survives the gray axis and black** (React Aria keeps `Color` objects of a color space; a Go `tela.Color` has no hue at gray): a color the caller passes back that equals the one reported is recognized (the exact HSB is kept), one that equals the one shown is the same color, and any other is converted keeping the shown hue when it is a gray and the saturation when it is black. `Set(tela.Color)` (a swatch, the hex field), `SetHSB`, `Color()`/`HSB()` (the last build) and `LiveColor()`/`LiveHSB()` (they subscribe the paint-phase closure that reads them, ADR-132's rule) are its methods; the hue slider's track and the area's background read the live color, so they follow a drag without a build. The hooks must be called in the component that made the state (its bump is that component's), and `State` is required by every hook (a missing one panics, with the fix). Drag builds are ADR-132's: an uncontrolled color builds when a drag starts and ends and when the pointer rests for 120 ms. **`UseColorArea(ColorAreaOptions{State, XChannel, YChannel, Direction, Disabled, OnChangeEnd(tela.Color), FormatValue, AriaLabel, AriaLabelledBy})`** (React Aria's `useColorArea`): `XChannel`/`YChannel` are any two different of `ColorHue`, `ColorSaturation` and `ColorBrightness` (saturation along X and brightness along Y by default; the third stays); the result has `AreaProps` (`group`, its name, the ref and the pointer handlers), `ThumbProps`, `AreaRef`, `ThumbRef`, `Dragging`, `ThumbPercent() (x, y float64)` (0 to 100 from the left and from the top, so brightness 100 is y 0; a `TranslateFn` reads it with `tela.UseSize(AreaRef)`) and `ValueText()`. A press on the area places the thumb at the point and drags it (a mouse is captured, touch and pen keep their implicit capture, a cancel ends the drag), a press on the thumb drags it by the pointer's movement (the grab is kept), each channel is rounded to its step (`snapValueToStep`); keys on the thumb: the arrows by one step (a page with Shift), PageUp and PageDown change **Y** by a page and Home and End change **X** by a page, as React Aria's area does (it has no jump to the limits), and `OnChangeEnd` follows every key that changed the color and every drag. **The area is mirrored in a right-to-left `Direction`** as React Aria's is (the X axis, the thumb and the left and right arrows; Home goes toward the left edge); the Y axis never is. The pages are React Aria's channel ranges: hue 0 to 360 by 1 and a page of 15, saturation, brightness and lightness 0 to 100 by 1 and a page of 10, alpha 0 to 1 by 0.01 and a page of 0.1. **The thumb is one `slider`** named by `AriaLabel` (else "Color area" or `AriaLabelledBy`) with the X channel's `aria-valuenow`, `-min` and `-max` and an `aria-valuetext` with both channels ("Saturation 58%, Brightness 94%"; `FormatValue(channel, v)` for another language, as English names are the default and the system locale is never read, ADR-132); React Aria has two hidden range inputs with `aria-roledescription="2D slider"`, which the semantics tree cannot express (no hidden inputs, no `aria-roledescription`; an engine change was not made for it). **`UseColorSlider(ColorSliderOptions{State, Channel, Space, Orientation, Direction, Disabled, OnChangeEnd, FormatValue, AriaLabel, AriaLabelledBy})`** (React Aria's `useColorSlider`) is a `UseSlider` with the channel's range, step and page, controlled by the shared state: `Channel` is required (`ColorHue`, `ColorAlpha`, `ColorSaturation`, `ColorBrightness`, `ColorLightness`) and `Space` (`"hsb"` by default, `"hsl"`) says which model the saturation and the lightness belong to (a brightness in `"hsl"` or a lightness in `"hsb"` panics in dev mode); the HSL saturation that a lightness of 0 or 100 hides is kept by the state, so that the lightness slider can leave black or white. The result has `Props`, `TrackProps`, `ThumbProps`, `TrackRef`, `ThumbRef`, `Channel`, `Min`, `Max`, `Step`, `Dragging`, `ThumbPercent()` (as the slider's, live), `Value()`, `ValueText()` ("Hue 180°", "Alpha 80%") and **`TrackColors() []tela.Color`**, the colors at the stops of the gradient behind the thumb from the channel's minimum to its maximum with the other channels of the color as they are now (the hue: 7 stops every 60 degrees; the lightness: 3; the others 2; the alpha from transparent to the opaque color), live, which is all the kit needs to draw the track; the name is the channel's English name unless `AriaLabelledBy` names it. `SliderOptions` gains `PageSize` (0 keeps a tenth of the range) for the hue's page of 15. **`UseColorField(ColorFieldOptions{State, Alpha, Disabled, ReadOnly, AriaLabel, AriaLabelledBy, Ref})`** (React Aria's `useColorField`) is a hex text field on `h.Input`: the result has `InputProps`, `LabelProps` (a click focuses the field), `InputValue`, `Editing` and `Ref`; while the field is not being edited it shows `FormatHexColor(state.Color(), Alpha)`, so a drag elsewhere updates it; typing makes a draft that changes nothing; Enter and a blur parse it with `ParseHexColor`: a code sets the color (a code without opacity keeps the current one, and with `Alpha` a code of 4 or 8 digits sets it) and anything else, an empty text too, reverts to the color's code. Characters that can never be part of a code are cancelled in `OnBeforeInput` (so the caret and the selection stay), more than 8 digits are refused in `OnChange`; ArrowUp and ArrowDown add or subtract one from the color as a 24-bit number, PageUp and PageDown sixteen (React Aria's field also has Home and End to the limits, which Tela leaves to the caret, and the mouse wheel). **Not ported:** `ColorWheel`, `ColorSwatchPicker` (the kit's swatches call `Set`), `ColorThumb`, color names and their descriptions in `aria-valuetext` ("dark vibrant blue"), localized channel names, the color spaces other than HSB and HSL (RGB channels), and form values. **Tests** (`aria/color*_test.go`): conversions (known values, a grid of 4096 colors round-tripped through HSB and HSL to the 8 bits of a code, the hue kept at saturation 0, out-of-gamut and NaN input), hex parsing errors, the area's corners and clamping, keys, right-to-left, other channels, touch and pen, the hue kept through gray and black, controlled refusal, aria snapshots, runnable examples, and `TestColorAreaDragDoesNotBuildPerMove` and `TestColorSliderDragDoesNotBuildPerMove` (a whole drag: 2 builds and 2 layouts, the thumb under the pointer after every move). **Names the owner has not seen:** `HSB`, `HSL`, `ToHSB`, `ToHSL`, `ParseHexColor`, `FormatHexColor`, `ColorChannel` and its constants `ColorHue`, `ColorSaturation`, `ColorBrightness`, `ColorLightness`, `ColorAlpha`, `UseColorState`, `ColorState`, `ColorStateOptions`, `LiveColor`, `LiveHSB`, `UseColorArea`, `ColorAreaOptions`, `ColorAreaResult`, `AreaProps`, `AreaRef`, `UseColorSlider`, `ColorSliderOptions`, `ColorSliderResult`, `TrackColors`, `Space`, `UseColorField`, `ColorFieldOptions`, `ColorFieldResult`, `PageSize` | A picker has three parts that change one color, and each of them alone would lose the hue (RGB has none at gray) or rebuild per move, so the color is one state that holds an HSB and publishes a version that paint closures read: the same mechanism as ADR-132's slider, shared instead of copied, and the reason `State` is a required argument as in react-stately. A `tela.Color` stays the picker's value type (ADR-129), with the conversions in `aria` where the hooks and the kit use them and the root package stays small. The color slider reuses `UseSlider` (rounding, keys, drag, roles, touch) and only maps its value to a channel; the area is the same drag with two axes, and its rules (page keys, mirroring, rounding) are React Aria's. A hex field that commits on blur and Enter and reverts bad input is what the owner's decision K10 asks of "a hex field", and sits on `h.Input` with `OnBeforeInput` (ADR-069) with no engine change. Rendering is unchanged: every golden of the repository is bit-identical |

| 137 | 2026-10-25 | `kit` overlays: Dialog, AlertDialog, Popover, Tooltip and the trigger-slot rule (phase 10, T5a; builds on ADR-129, ADR-130 and the hooks of ADR-101). **The trigger-slot rule (one for every kit overlay, menu, select and picker).** A `Trigger` slot is a `kit.Trigger`: a component with one method, `WithTriggerProps(tela.Prop) tela.Component`, that returns itself with the props of the trigger merged onto its root element after its own (Radix's `asChild`: the overlay does not wrap the trigger, so the button the app wrote is the one with the press, `aria-haspopup`, `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`, the ref the overlay is placed against and the hover of a tooltip). `kit.Button` implements it (an unexported `trigger` field applied after `Props`); an app's own component implements it with the method; any other element is made one with `kit.TriggerFunc(func(p tela.Prop) tela.Node)`, which is called as a component of its own, so it may use hooks. A type that is not a `Trigger` does not compile in the slot, and an empty slot (nil) opens the overlay from its state alone. Only one overlay can sit on one trigger element (each puts its own `h.Ref` there, and two refs panic in dev mode, ADR-101). **Dialog** `kit.Dialog{Trigger, Title, Description, Children, Footer, ShowClose, CloseIcon, CloseLabel, AriaLabel, Open *bool, DefaultOpen, OnOpenChange, Class, OverlayClass, HeaderClass, TitleClass, DescriptionClass, FooterClass, CloseClass, Props}` is `UseOverlayTrigger`, a dismissable modal `UseOverlay` and `UseDialog` in a `Portal` and a `Presence`: the page is inert, Tab is contained, Escape and a press on the scrim close it, the focus is restored; the panel exists only while open, so its state starts afresh; the description is the dialog's `aria-describedby` (`h.AriaDescribedBy`). `ShowClose` is off by default (shadcn's `showCloseButton` is on): the zero value of a Go bool cannot default to true, and the owner named the field `ShowClose`; the X is Lucide's, declared in `dialog.go` as an unexported `tela.Icon` (ISC, `THIRD_PARTY.md`) and replaceable with `CloseIcon`. **AlertDialog** adds `Cancel`/`Action` (label slots, "Cancel" and "Continue" by default, drawn as `kit.Button`s: outline and default or destructive), `Destructive`, `OnAction`, `OnCancel` and `Size` (default or `Sm`, the pinned commit's `size`); role `alertdialog`, not dismissable by a press outside (Escape closes it, without calling either handler), first focus on Cancel. shadcn's `AlertDialogMedia` is not ported. **Popover** `kit.Popover{Trigger, Children, Placement, Align, Offset, Modal, AriaLabel, Open..., Class, Props}`: `UsePopover` plus `UseDialog` on one ref (role `dialog`, the focus goes in and comes back), non-modal by default, `Placement` a side (`bottom`) or a full Floating UI placement, `Align` `start`/`center`/`end` (a typo panics in dev mode with the closest), `Offset` 0 meaning shadcn's 4 px and `kit.NoOffset` (-1) none. **Tooltip** `kit.Tooltip{Trigger, Content, Placement (top), Align, Offset (0), Delay, Disabled, Open..., Class, ArrowClass, Props}`: `UseTooltipTrigger` with `Delay` 0 meaning none (shadcn's provider default; `aria`'s 1.5 s is `Delay: 1500*time.Millisecond`), the close delay and the group warm-up of `aria` (per window, so no provider is needed), the arrow of `UseFloating` sunk 2 px into the tooltip (Radix puts the arrow's center 2 px inside the edge, Floating UI on it) by a per-side margin of the recipe. **Recipes** (`kit.Style` gains `AlertDialog`, `Dialog`, `Popover`, `Tooltip`): `DialogStyle{Positioner, Overlay, OverlayExit, Root, Exit, Header, Title, Description, Footer, Close, CloseIcon}` (`Root` is the dialog element; shadcn centers it with `fixed top-[50%] left-[50%]` and a translation, here the `Positioner`, a fixed flex layer with `p-4`, centers it, which gives the same box and keeps the panel's own transform for the entrance; the scrim is `absolute inset-0` inside it), `AlertDialogStyle` (the same, `Sizes`, `HeaderSizes`, `FooterSizes` for the `data-[size=...]` classes), `PopoverStyle{Root, Exit, Sides}` and `TooltipStyle{Root, Exit, Sides, Arrow, ArrowSides}`, where `Sides` (keyed `top`, `right`, `bottom`, `left`) holds the transform origin and the 0.5rem slide-in start of shadcn's `data-[side=...]:slide-in-from-*`, chosen by the side the element is on. Entrances are `transition` plus `starting:` classes and exits are `Exit` classes in a `Presence` (`animate-in`/`animate-out` become transitions of 150 ms, 200 ms for dialogs). **Deviations:** the classes of `DialogContent` are shadcn's minus its positioning; the max width is `max-w-lg` (the 1 rem of room on each side is the positioner's padding, so below `sm` the panel is `100% - 2rem` wide as shadcn's `max-w-[calc(100%-2rem)]`), so a `sm:max-w-[425px]` ported from shadcn's docs still wins above `sm`; the close button's `data-[state=open]:` classes (Radix's Close has no state) are dropped; `text-balance` parses as plain wrapping (the engine does not balance lines; the Chromium cells of wrapped tooltips leave it out of the markup); the markup is shadcn's `cn()` output without the positioning and `data-[state=...]` classes; the harness `cn` gains the `max-w` group): sheets `dialog` (5 cells), `alertdialog` (4), `popover` (3) and `tooltip` (4) in light and dark at scales 1 and 2: box edges differ by at most 0.012 px at x1 and 0.004 px at x2 (Dialog, AlertDialog and Popover 0.000); mean pixel difference per channel around a cell, worst cell at x1/x2: Dialog 1.14/2.12, AlertDialog 1.01/1.03, Popover 1.63/1.35, Tooltip 4.00/1.72 (three lines of white text on black, bound 4.5 at x1); plus window goldens of each overlay open (`<name>_open_<scheme>_x<scale>.png`), behavior tests in telatest (open and close by pointer and keyboard, Escape, outside press, inert page, Tab containment, focus restore, controlled and uncontrolled, the three ways to make a trigger, placement with flip and shift, delay, warm-up, keyboard focus, arrow pixels, aria snapshots), runnable examples, and idle tests: an open overlay requests no frames once its entrance ends. | The owner's slots (ADR-129) need the overlay to reach the element the app wrote, and a method on the component is the shortest rule that keeps types honest and stays out of the engine (no way to apply a `Prop` to an arbitrary `Node` exists, and a wrapper element would put the role, the tab stop and the name on the wrong box); A value no item has chooses nothing, so the zero `K` shows the placeholder (a select whose keys include the zero value has it chosen: documented). Inside a `Field` it takes the field's id, description and invalid state, and the field's label text is its `AriaLabel` (`fieldInfo` gained `label`), so its name is `value + label` as React Aria's. **Tabs[K]** (`aria.UseTabs`): `Tabs []Tab[K]{Value, Label, Icon, Disabled, Content}`, the value rule, `Orientation`, `Variant` (Default, and `kit.TabsLine`, a component constant since ADR-130 limits the shared `Variant` ones), `ManualActivation`, `AriaLabel`; only the selected panel is built. **Toaster / UseToast:** `kit.Toaster{Position, Duration, VisibleToasts, Expand, CloseButton, Inline, Icons, CloseIcon, Children}` once around the app; `kit.UseToast() ToastHandle` (a comparable struct holding the store pointer) with `Show`, `Success`, `Error`, `Info`, `Warning`, `Loading` (all return a `ToastID`), `Dismiss(id)` and `DismissAll`, options `ToastDescription`, `ToastAction(label, onClick)`, `ToastDuration` (`ToastForever` < 0), `ToastReplace(id)` (sonner's `id`: update in place and restart the timer), `ToastOnDismiss` and `ToastOnAutoClose`; six `ToastPosition`s (bottom right by default). The state is a mutex-guarded store in the Toaster (`UseOnce`) that every method changes from any goroutine and then asks the Toaster to rebuild with a `tela.Setter` bump, which routes through the tree like `tela.Post`; callbacks run on the UI thread through `tela.Post`; the context value is the stable store pointer, so the app below does not rebuild when a toast comes or goes; there is no global. **Look:** sonner's default stylesheet as shadcn's wrapper leaves it (356 px, 14 px gap, 24 px offset, 16 px padding, 13 px text, the popover colors, the 4 px 12 px shadow, 400 ms transitions), the newest toast in front and the older ones behind it at `translateY(14 n px) scale(1 - 0.05 n)` with their content hidden and the front's height, three shown, fanned out by their measured heights while the pointer is over the viewport (or with `Expand`), entering and leaving by `starting:` and `Exit` classes from the edge; each toast measures its body with `tela.UseSize` and tells the store, the cards take explicit heights so they transition. A toast is `status` (an `alert` for errors) inside a `region` named Notifications. **Timer:** the public API has `UseInterval` and no one-shot timer nor clock read, so a toast counts a 100 ms `UseInterval` in a ref (paused with a period of 0 while the pointer is over the stack: sonner's remaining-time rule); ticks change no state, so an idle waiting toast draws no frame (tested), and `Advance` runs it deterministically. **Deviations from shadcn and sonner:** the select list is shadcn's `position="popper"` (below the trigger, centered, at least as wide as it, 4 px away; shadcn's default `item-aligned` over the trigger is not ported) and has no scroll buttons (it scrolls inside a height limited to the room, `aria.Size`); the list's `relative` is dropped (its props fix it to the window); | 143 | 2026-10-25 | `kit` color picker (phase 10, T9, kit half; builds on ADR-129 K9, ADR-130, ADR-135, ADR-136 and ADR-137; shadcn/ui has no color picker, so the look is the kit's own design in shadcn's language, not a port, and nothing is added to `THIRD_PARTY.md`). **API.** `ColorPicker{Value *tela.Color, DefaultValue, OnValueChange func(tela.Color), OnValueCommit func(tela.Color), Alpha, Swatches []tela.Color, Disabled, AreaLabel, HueLabel, AlphaLabel, HexLabel, Class, Props}` is the inline panel (16rem wide, the room inside a `Popover`'s `p-4` and `w-72`): an area for saturation and brightness, a hue slider, an alpha slider when `Alpha`, a row with a preview and the hex field, and a row of swatch buttons when `Swatches` is not empty. The value rule is ADR-101's; **the zero `DefaultValue` is opaque black** (the zero `tela.Color` is transparent, which every color the picker made from it would be); `OnValueChange` is aria's `OnChange` (every change, also while dragging, so a controlled picker builds as often as its state changes, ADR-132), and **`OnValueCommit` is called once when the user stops** (a drag ends, a key, a swatch or a committed hex code changed the color), never for a color equal to the last committed one; the names that default to English (`Color area`, `Hue`, `Alpha`, `Hex`; the swatches are named by their code) are strings the app sets for another language. `ColorInput{<the color fields of ColorPicker>, Disabled, Trigger kit.Trigger, Placement, Align ("start"), Offset, Open *bool, DefaultOpen, OnOpenChange, AriaLabel, <the four labels>, Class, PanelClass, PopoverClass, Props}` is the swatch button (an outline `Button` with a swatch and the hex text) that opens the picker in a `Popover`: **decided as a component of its own** rather than a `Trigger` field of `ColorPicker`, because the two differ in what they own (a picker that is a popover's content exists only while open; the color has to outlive it and the button has to show it) and a form field that shows a color is what apps ask for by name (`kit.Select` has the same shape); `Trigger` follows ADR-137's rule (the default is a `Button`, any other trigger is the app's and gets the popover's props, and shows nothing of the color unless the app draws it from `OnValueChange`). **Built on the hooks of ADR-135** (`UseColorState`, `UseColorArea`, `UseColorSlider` for the hue and the alpha, `UseColorField` on the kit's `Input` classes): one `useColorParts` hook in the component that owns the color (`ColorPicker`, or `ColorInput`, which calls the hooks whether its panel is open or not and gives the results to the `Popover`'s content, drawn by a `colorPanel` component with no hooks of its own), because the state asks *its* component to build when a drag starts and ends and the hooks must be in it (a first version that made the controls components of their own showed it: the field kept the color of the last build after a slider drag, and the fix is this structure); every hook is called whatever the props are (the alpha slider's too, drawn or not) so `Alpha` and `Disabled` may change. **Recipes** (`Style` gains `ColorInput` and `ColorPicker`; part names are public API): `ColorPickerStyle{Root, Disabled, Area, AreaSaturation, AreaBrightness, AreaThumb, Slider, Track, HueTrack, AlphaTrack, AlphaFill, Thumb, Checkerboard, Row, Preview, Input, Swatches, Swatch, SwatchSelected}` and `ColorInputStyle{Trigger, Swatch}`. The area is the hue at full saturation and brightness (painted live by `BgFn`) with `bg-linear-to-r from-white to-transparent` and `bg-linear-to-t from-black to-transparent` over it, as every HSV picker is drawn; the thumb is a `size-5` circle with a white border, `shadow-md` and the kit slider's `ring-ring/50` on hover and focus, filled with the color live; the sliders have `h-3` rounded tracks, the same thumb at `size-4`, and thumbs **centered on the value** (they hang 8 px over the ends of the track, unlike the kit `Slider`'s, which stays inside it, so the thumb is where the pointer pressed and above the color it stands for); the hex field is `h.Input` with `Input.Root` and the hook's props, committing on Enter and blur and reverting what is not a code (aria's rules); a swatch is a button with `aria-pressed` for the color that is current, and its `SwatchSelected` ring. **Checkerboard** (the pattern under the alpha track, the preview and the swatches): `bg-[conic-gradient(#d4d4d4_25%,#fff_0_50%,#d4d4d4_0_75%,#fff_0)] bg-[length:12px_12px]` with a dark-scheme pair (`#404040`, `#262626`), a CSS conic gradient that repeats every 12 px, with no image to draw and nothing to scale (an SVG pattern was not needed). **Dragging does not build** (`TestColorPickerDragRepaintsOnly`, 20 moves of one drag each of the area, the hue and the alpha: 0 builds, 0 layouts, 20 paints; the drag's end is 1 build; the same for a drag inside a `ColorInput`'s open panel): the area's background, the thumbs and their fills, the preview and the trigger's swatch are paint-phase functions of `LiveHSB`/`LiveColor`. Two things cannot follow a drag without a build because a gradient has no paint-phase form (`BackgroundGradient` takes a value): the **alpha track's** gradient from clear to the color, drawn from the color of the last build (it catches up when a drag of the area or the hue rests for 120 ms or ends), and the text of a `ColorInput`'s button. **Deviation from ADR-135's hint:** the hue track is the fixed rainbow (`bg-linear-[90deg,#f00,#ff0,#0f0,#0ff,#00f,#f0f,#f00]/srgb`), not `TrackColors()`, which carries the color's saturation and brightness: a gray color would make the hue slider gray and the user could not see which hue to choose, and it would be one more gradient that needs a build; `TrackColors` stays for apps that want it. **Disabled `ColorInput`:** `kit.Popover` has no disabled state and its trigger props (`aria.UseOverlayTrigger`'s `buttonProps(false)`) re-enable the button they are merged into, so a disabled `ColorInput` draws its trigger without the popover (ADR-145 found the same gap in the examples; a `Disabled` field of `Popover` would be the general fix and is open for the owner). **Conformance** (`kit/colorgradients_test.go`, a sheet whose cells are the area, the hue track and the alpha track drawn from the recipe's classes with the colors the component paints, against Chromium CSS gradients of the same colors with Tailwind 4.3.3, light and dark, scales 1 and 2; 5 cells): box edges 0.000 px, mean pixel difference per channel 0.20 to 0.22 (area), 0.12 to 0.20 (hue) and 0.21 to 0.28 (alpha, over the conic checkerboard), bound 0.5. Two findings, both about the engine: **an arbitrary `bg-[linear-gradient(...)]` interpolates in Oklab** where CSS defaults to sRGB (the mask images use sRGB, `bg-linear-*` follows Tailwind's `in oklab`), which made the rainbow differ from Chromium's by 5.5 levels and the colors between the stops wrong for a hue (an HSV hue is sRGB-linear between the 60-degree stops); `bg-linear-[...]/srgb` gives the CSS result and Chromium gets the CSS it stands for in an inline style, since Tailwind has no spelling for it; and the parser does not read `in srgb` inside a gradient (`bg-[linear-gradient(to_right_in_srgb,...)]`, which Tailwind passes through): open for the owner. **Other tests:** goldens of the picker (default, alpha with swatches, a gray color, a selected swatch, disabled) and of the `ColorInput` (four closed states, and the open window with the panel), light and dark at scales 1 and 2; behavior (area, hue and alpha by mouse, touch and pen with a cancel and a drag off the area; keys per aria; the hex field committing on Enter and blur, reverting a bad code and keeping the opacity of a short one; swatches and their `aria-pressed`; the hue kept through black and gray; controlled with a refused change and an outside change; disabled; labels; aria snapshots; popover open and close, focus into the panel and back to the trigger, Escape, an outside press, a custom `Trigger`, a controlled `Open` and `Value`; the color kept when the panel closes); idle (no frames after a drag, nor with the panel open); runnable examples. Touch and pen go through a small headless injector in `kit/colorpicker_touch_test.go` because `telatest` has no touch action. Every existing golden is bit-identical | K9 asks for a picker that is a few controls over one color, and ADR-135's hooks make each control a repaint-only widget; the structure that follows is that the component that owns the color owns the hooks, which is why a popover version needs its own owner, and why the picker is not `Popover` plus `ColorPicker` glued by `OnValueChange` (that would build the owner on every move). The look keeps what shadcn gives every control (tokens, radius, the `ring-ring/50` focus, `shadow-xs`, the kit `Input` for the hex field and the kit `Slider`'s thumb treatment) and adds what a color needs, the conventions of every picker people know (white and black gradients over the hue, a rainbow, a checkerboard under what is transparent). Checking the gradients against Chromium is what found that the engine interpolates arbitrary gradients in Oklab |
